2027 price hike should be case by case amid push back from some customers:
Management said 2027 pricing negotiations could be mixed across customers even
though capacity is nearly fully loaded. Some need more capacity in 4Q26 and are
willing to pay the new post-hike wafer price. However, the company still faces push
back from some customers, likely tied to their inventories. Our recent checks
suggest  Tier 1 foundry L TA wafer pricing into 2027 could be less than 10%, below

the buyside bogey of a 25-30% blended hike. However, for some tier 2 customers,
the price hike could indeed be around 25-30%. We believe the average like-to-like
L TA wafer price could only increase by  15-20% in 2027. Stay EW.
SOI demand exceeds expectation: The company said 12-inch SOI demand already
exceeds its current expansion plan. The priority is to debottleneck and ramp existing
Missouri capacity. More than half of the newly installed tools have qualified for

volume production, and RF SOI and silicon-photonics products have entered volume
production.
4Q26 could be relatively muted: We expect 4Q26 to be relatively muted since
wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is fully loaded, and
new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin.
With this report, Lucas Wang assumes coverage of GlobalWafers.

relatively muted since wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is
fully loaded and new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin. We had

expected 4Q26 gross margin to be around 25-26%, but we have now reduced that to 22-
23%.

Micron/GWC deal marks a new milestone for GWC
Micron Technology and GlobalWafers announced a deeper strategic partnership on July 9,

2026, centered on a 10-year supply agreement and US$500mn of strategic financing from
Micron to support GlobalWafers' new 12-inch silicon wafer facility in Sherman, Texas. On
its latest earnings call, GWC board proposed executing that financing as up to US$500
million of 10-year privately placed convertible bonds, with Micron as the sole subscriber,
carrying a 0.5% coupon. The agreement secures significant raw silicon wafer capacity for
Micron's long-term manufacturing plans, with the financing linked to the buildout of

GlobalWafers America's (GWA) Sherman facility.
GlobalWafers Chairwoman Doris Hsu described the agreement as the largest and longest
contract in the company's history. The two companies will also collaborate on next-
generation wafer technologies and process innovation. The Sherman plant is notable as
the only US-based source of advanced 12-inch wafers.
The agreement forms part of Micron's broader US$3bn commitment to strengthen the US
semiconductor supply chain and accompanies its target of manufacturing 40% of its

DRAM output domestically by the mid-2030s. We believe GWC is well positioned to

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
support ongoing foundry and memory capacity expansion in the US, not only for Micron
but also for TSMC and Samsung, which are already GWC's two largest customers. We
currently assume Micron/GWC L TA pricing could be US$70-80 on average for the polished
wafer (10-20% above current L TA pricing after discounts), reflecting the higher US cost
base. This would be broadly in line with, or slightly above, L TAs signed in 2021-22.
Current inventory levels remain higher than during the 2021-22 upcycle

when existing L TAs were signed
Notably, customer inventories of 12-inch wafers remain well above levels seen during the
previous industry upcycle. According to SUMCO's inventory trend data (see Exhibit 3 ),
inventory volumes and turnover days were near the lowest levels of the 2014-26 period
during 2021-22, reflecting a lean and highly utilized supply chain. By contrast, the current
cycle presents the opposite picture: shipment volumes are approaching record highs, but

customer inventories are also near record levels, with inventory days and absolute
inventory exceeding those seen even at the peak of the 2021-22 upcycle.
This backdrop differs meaningfully from the 2021-22 environment and, in our view,
supports a more moderate and delayed pricing recovery in 2027 rather than a repeat of
the broad-based increases seen during the prior cycle. In 2021-22, wafer makers held
significant pricing leverage as customer inventories sat near cycle lows despite record

shipment volumes. Fabs consumed nearly all purchased wafers with minimal inventory
buffers, creating a tight supply environment that enabled wafer manufacturers to
implement meaningful price increases across 12-inch wafers.
While shipment volumes currently remain elevated, customers also hold substantial
inventory. This provides a buffer against demand growth and moderate supply disruptions,
reducing the urgency to secure incremental wafer supply. Historically, this dynamic

weakens suppliers' pricing power. Until inventories normalize meaningfully, wafer makers
are likely to have less pricing leverage than they did entering the 2021-22 upcycle. Our
view is that 2027 L TA negotiations will focus primarily on removing current discounts
rather than driving nominal price increases. We broadly expect 2027 industry L TA pricing
increases of 15-20% in average, with Tier 2 customers' wafer price to be raised 25-30% but
Tier 1 customers would only see 5-15%.

Estimate Revisions
We cut our EPS estimates by 14%, 15% and 14% for 2026-28, respectively. The
reductions in our  forecasts primarily reflect lower wafer price hike to Tier 1 customers and
the margin pressure from overseas capacity ramp. However, we also lower our tax rate
assumption from 25% to 20% to reflect the subsidy from the US government.

Morgan Stanley Research 7
Valuation Methodology
We lower our price target from NT$900 to NT$770: The price target change mostly
reflects our earnings forecast change over  2026-28. All of of key assumptions in our DCF
model are unchanged, including  a cost of equity of 11% (beta of 1.5, risk premium of 6%
and risk-free rate of 2%), an intermediate growth rate of 13%  and a terminal growth rate
of 4%.
Our bull- and bear-case values are also lowered, from  NT$1,140.00 to NT$975.00 and NT

$450.00 to NT$385.00, respectively, implying P/Es of 32x and 13x 2027e.

NT$975.00
32x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers faster than
expected. Pure foundry players' utilization
rates rebound to 100%, and memory players
start to pull in more raw wafers. GWC's
total revenue CAGR picks up to >30% in
2025-28 vs. 8% in 2021-23. Gross margin
recovers to over 40% vs. 37.4% in 2023.
NT$770.00
25x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers from a

down-cycle. Pure foundry and memory
players start to see recovery in utilization
rate, while both spot price and LTA amount
also increase gradually. GWC's total revenue
CAGR rises to >15% in 2025-28 vs. 8% in
2021-23. Gross margin stays above 25%.
NT$385.00
13x 2027e EPS
Semiconductor industry recovers more
slowly than expected during this down-
cycle. Pure foundry players start to breach
LTAs and memory players start to cut more
raw wafer orders. GWC's total revenue

CAGR slows to 0% in 2025-28 vs. 8% in
2021-23. Gross margin declines to <20% vs.
37.4% in 2023.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Micron Technology Inc. (MU.O)
30x through-cycle earnings of US $40.00, a premium to history reflecting new opportunities
in AI, in-line with broader semis.
Risks to Upside
n Customers continue to demonstrate an appetite to take on inventory around macroeco-
nomic uncertainty
n Additional wafer intensity of HBM further improves overall supply and demand
n MIcron's HBM share surpasses expectations
Risks to Downside

n Pricing can turn quickly; a falter in end demand with inventories elevated could lead to
a swift price reduction
n HBM demand falters and competition intensifies, pressuring pricing

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Greater China  Semiconductors  | Asia Pacific
PC Semis: More divergent
outlooks
Business diversification, KGD (known good die) pricing power
and supply chain management create a divergent revenue
outlook for PC semis into 4Q and likely also 1Q27. Downgrade
Elan to EW.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
Elan Microelectronics Corp
(2458.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$250.00 NT$160.00
A much tougher 4Q… We mentioned in the prior PC semis note that the FOMP
(fear of missing procurement) could lead to  stable PC semis shipments in 3Q; now,

the inventory accumulation in 1Q-3Q26 could cause a deeper correction in 4Q.  PC
semis customers currently still aim for only a 5-10% Q/Q procurement decline in 4Q.
That said, the downside surprise in Parade’s 4Q outlook (link), which calls for a 20-
25% Q/Q revenue decline, could suggest a worse component correction. The still-
elevated Windows activation period of 200+ days and much smaller price hikes
from PC semis customers in 2H vs. 1H could suggest ongoing weakness into 1Q27.

But why are some companies resilient? Many investors are asking why Realtek’s
revenue could be up Q/Q in 4Q amid the PC correction (link). We cite three factors:
1) Consumer demand (other than PC/smartphones) is not necessarily all bad,
especially for IoT devices (low memory content). Espressif is guiding for  Q/Q
revenue growth in 4Q, following sequential growth in 3Q. 2) Price hikes reflect
increases at both  foundries/OSATs and in KGD. For example, TV SoC pricing is

several times higher than in past years due to  KGD price hikes (mainly DDR4). 3)
Large-scale IC design houses usually have better supply chain management and
lower exposure to PC.
No new growth driver into 2027: With the recent debate on cloud AI, we think it’s
still a matter of time before edge AI  takes off. However, we haven’t seen a clear
growth driver for PC semis. Preliminary outlooks from PC semis customers range
from flattish to down 20% Y/Y in 2027. Thus, it's  difficult to turn positive now.

Reduce earnings: We revise down Elan's PT and earnings and cut the stock to EW.
We appreciate the company's growth from smart Edge AI devices, but its exposure
to NB is still too significant to overlook. Our PT implies 16x our downward-revised
2027 EPS. Stay EW  Realtek and UW  Novatek, ASMedia, Parade, and Nuvoton.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
The FOMP (fear of missing procurement for PC semis in 1Q-3Q26) has caused a
deeper correction for some PC semis into 4Q.
It's easier for large-scale PC semis  to navigate through the downturn, while small
design houses are more concentrated on PC.
We stay conservative on PC semis, with no OW and await the cycle bottom

sometime in 2027.
November 3, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

2
Elan Micro: Earnings estimate revisions
We revise our EPS estimates by +1%/-20%/-16% for 2026/27/28: For 2026, we
incorporate actual 2Q26 results, which benefited from investment gains. For 2027-28, we
reduce our revenue and GM forecasts due to continued weak PC demand, which we
believe cannot be fully offset by the non PC business (mainly the drone market).
Exhibit 1: Elan: Estimate revisions
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (e) estimates.

Exhibit 2: Elan: Quarterly financial statement
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (e) estimates
Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$160: This reflects our earnings estimate revisions for
2026-28. Our price target is our base-case scenario value, which we continue to derive
from our residual income model. We reduce our intermediate growth rate estimate from
10.0% to 8.0% to reflect a weak PC business outlook. We reduce our terminal growth rate

estimate from 4.5% to 3.0% to better align it with broader economic growth. We continue
to assume a cost of equity of 9.6% and a payout ratio of 80%.
Our bull case value is cut to NT$234 from NT$366, and our bear case value is cut to NT
$84 from NT$131.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Update
Winbond Electronics Corp  | Asia Pacific
Strong 3Q margins; growth to
moderate in 4Q
3Q26 earnings beat: 3Q revenue was NT$82bn, +38% Q/Q, +278% Y/Y, 8% below
MSe; GM was 70.6%, +4.3ppt Q/Q, +23.9ppt Y/Y, 12.5ppt above MSe; EPS was NT
$7.49, +39% Q/Q, +1045% Y/Y, 3% above MSe. CMS revenue grew 50% Q/Q as ASP
increased by ~60%, despite lower bit shipments, which was due to reserving supply
for L TA customers. Flash revenue rose 34% Q/Q on pricing and mix with flat bit

shipments. Memory GM reached 73.9%.
4Q growth to moderate; 16nm supports 2027: Management expects continued 4Q
growth, but a slower pace as non-AI demand softens. DRAM contract prices should
rise more moderately than in 3Q, while high density NOR remains tight. KH module
A capacity should reach 24-25kwpm by end-2026. 16nm ramp from ~2kwpm to
16kwpm is expected to support 2H27 shipments.
AI opportunities broaden; module  B capex approved: Management projects server

NOR bit shipments to grow 67% Y/Y in 2027. CUBE customers are increasingly
inference accelerator developers with several expected to begin small volume
production in 2027. Silicon capacitor capacity is largely allocated, but current
margins remain below conventional DRAM. The board newly approved NT$73bn
capex for the previously announced Module B expansion.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Winbond Electronics Corp (2344.TW)
Base case, 2.9x 2027e P/B. This method is in line with the approach we adopt for Greater China
memory IDM peers, in view of the industry's high volatility. We believe the stock merits a mul-
tiple beyond the high end of its historical band of 0.5-1.5x since 2017, as we are seeing DRAM
pricing upside, a sustainable NOR price hike, and opportunities in SLC NAND and SiCap
foundry business.

Risks to Upside
n NOR Flash pricing increase, driven by stronger-than-expected demand
n Stronger-than-expected DRAM pricing given ongoing supply shortage
n Faster-than-expected SLC NAND development
Risks to Downside
n NOR Flash downcycle, driven by weaker demand
n Lower-than-expected DRAM pricing given ongoing oversupply
n Slower-than-expected SLC NAND development

## Document 209: [000270] 기아(000270) 잠시 쉬어갑니다기아(000270) 잠시 쉬어갑니다잠시 쉬어갑니다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 잠시 쉬어갑니다기아(000270) 잠시 쉬어갑니다잠시 쉬어갑니다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.11.02  
잠시 쉬어갑니다

3Q26 Review 
기아는 3Q26P 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.4% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회했다. 영업이익 증
감 요인은 환율 효과(+5,290억원), 물량 효과(+4,420억원), 믹스 효과(-4,650억원), 
인센티브/가격 효과(-7,270억원), 재료비 영향(-3,680억원), 기타(+4,130억원)이다. 
판매 성장에도 높아진 수익성 부담 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.9% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.1만대
(+8.8%)를 기록했다. 미국 HEV와 서유럽 EV 판매가 각각 118%, 163% 증가하며 
글로벌 점유율은 4.0%까지 확대됐다. 다만, 판매 성장에도 원화 강세와 원자재 가격 
상승, EV 비중 확대에 따른 믹스 부담으로 수익성은 전분기 대비 하락했다. 유럽 내 
EV 판매가 늘어나면서 믹스가 악화되었으며, 전쟁으로 인한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 
재료비 부담은 전년 대비 약 3,700억 증가했다.  
연간 판매목표 335만대는 유지했으나, 4분기 수익성의 급격한 반등은 어려울 전망이
다. 향후 미국 텔루라이드·스포티지 HEV 공급 확대와 유럽 EV 판매 증가에 따른 고정
비 흡수가 수익성 회복의 핵심이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 155,000원으로 하향 
기아에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 실적 추정치 하향을 반영해 목표주가를 
155,000원으로 하향한다. 재료비 부담, 환율 효과 축소, 예상보다 큰 믹스 악화로 단
기 이익 눈높이 조정은 불가피하다. 다만 산업수요 둔화에도 주요 시장에서 점유율을 
확대되고 있다는 점은 긍정적이다. 현재 배당 수익률은 7% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높
은 배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 251: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02.pdf

파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 
4분기 성장 확대 
홍가혜

kahye0.hong@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 2,000,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.21) 
1,417,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
악조건 속에서도 이 정도 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 영업실적은 매출액 5조 7,536억원(yoy +30.2%, qoq 
`-9.1%), 영업이익 8,992억원(yoy +61.3%, qoq -13.5%)으로 당사 추정치에 대체로 
부합하였다. 조업일수 감소와 원화 강세로 매출액이 감소했으며, 임단협 타결로 
인한 성과금 및 격려금이 당분기에 비용으로 반영되었다. 해양플랜트 부문에서는 
RUYA 프로젝트의 공사 진행률 확대와 TRION 프로젝트의 C/O(계약 변경) 
180억원이 추가로 반영되며 매출액이 전분기대비 0.7% 증가하였다. 엔진기계 
부문은 환율 하락을 판가 상승과 D/F(이중연료) 비중 증가로 상쇄하며 전분기와 
유사한 영업실적을 달성했다. 
2027년에도 지속될 수주 시점 효과 
3분기 대형선 야드의 수주 시점별 매출 비중은 2022년 4%, 2023년 45%, 2024년 
31%, 2025년 20% 수준으로 파악된다. 상대적으로 선가가 낮았던 2022~2023년 
물량이 여전히 매출의 절반 가량을 차지하고 있는 셈이다. 따라서 2027년 말까지도 
고선가 물량의 매출 비중 확대가 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이는 LNG운반선 매출 
비중 감소에도 불구하고 수익성을 방어할 수 있는 핵심 요인으로 작용할 전망이다.   
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 550,000원으로 하향 
투자의견은 매수로 유지하나, 목표주가는 550,000원으로 34.5% 하향한다. 환율 
가정을 기존 1,450원 수준에서 1,400원으로 조정함에 따라 목표주가 산정의 기준이 
되는 2027~2028년 실적추정치를 하향조정하였다. 또한 미국 네오 클라우드 
업체들의 자금 조달 여건 악화로 데이터센터 증설이 지연되고 있어, 발전용 엔진 
증설을 근거로 동사에 부여하였던 프리미엄을 제거하여 P/E 배수를 19.8배로 
하향조정했다. 그러나 본업인 상선 부문은 향후 고부가가치 가스선의 발주 강세를 
전망하기 때문에 투자의견은 매수로 유지한다.

## Document 280: AI泡沫破裂了吗？高盛：当下更像1998年，而非2000年

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI泡沫破裂了吗？高盛：当下更像1998年，而非2000年.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛交易台表⽰，当下这波抛售更像 1998 年流动性危机，⽽⾮ 2000 年泡沫破裂，是杠杆强
制出清⽽⾮商业逻辑被证伪。市场剧烈重定价的是建设融资与时间节奏，⽽⾮底层需求。
此外，市场⽬前最关注的电⼒与许可问题恰恰是最不重要的⼀条，供给受限并不等于需求
消失。真正需要回答的问题是：收⼊端能否最终证明所投⼊资本的合理性。
摘要：
科技股遭遇剧烈抛售，但究竟是杠杆出清还是商业模式被证伪，这决定了截然相反的操作⽅
向。
据追⻛交易台，⾼盛 FICC 与股票交易台在 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇报告中表⽰，纳斯达克指数单⽇下跌 5.5% ，
费城半导体指数重挫 12% ，标普 500 指数下跌 4.4% 。⾼盛现⾦股票业务数据显⽰，当⽇美国现
⾦股票业务净卖出 30 亿美元，其中信息技术与通信服务板块占 22 亿美元，科技板块累计净卖出
规模已扩⼤⾄ 270 亿美元，超过 7 ⽉那轮 210 亿美元的技术性抛售。与此同时， VVIX 单⽇变动幅
度为 2018 年 2 ⽉ 5 ⽇ " 波动率末⽇ " 以来最⼤。

⾼盛 FICC 与股票业务负责⼈ Rich Privorotsky 在上述⽇更中明确表态，倾向于将本轮下跌定性为
类似 1998 年的流动性危机，⽽⾮ 2000 年科技泡沫破裂的前兆。他的核⼼判断是：AI 增⻓引擎本
⾝可能依然完好，市场剧烈重定价的是建设融资与时间节奏，⽽⾮底层需求。这⼀判断对投资
者的操作含义⾄关重要 —— 若类⽐成⽴，历史上此类插曲最终都演变为买⼊机会。
杠杆⾃我强化，⽽⾮估值重估
本轮抛售的机制特征指向强制去杠杆，⽽⾮基本⾯驱动的估值重估。
Privorotsky 指出，杠杆 ETF 在收盘前的强制平仓放⼤了半导体与纳斯达克的跌幅，叠加做市商负
gamma 敞⼝形成⾃我强化的清算循环。从资⾦流向看，⾼盛主经纪业务数据显⽰，半导体板块
的核⼼压⼒在于⻓线机构（ LO ）基础需求的缺席，多策略基⾦、量化基⾦、⻓线机构与散⼾呈
现同向卖出， " 所有⼈站在同⼀边 " 本⾝即是问题所在。
相关性上升、动量因⼦跑输、残余杠杆因⼦持续抛售，⽽低⻉塔、防御与质量资产获得追捧
—— 这⼀组合读数与典型的强制降⻛险特征⾼度吻合。从选举后两⽇的市场演变来看，第⼀⽇ AI

股跑输⽽周期股因政治中⼼化预期反弹，第⼆⽇则是剧烈的补跌：若数据中⼼建设⼤幅放缓，
名义增⻓预期将⾯临下修，周期股的反弹本⾝便存在重新定价的压⼒。
1998 还是 2000 ：两种模板，两种结局
Privorotsky 将本轮下跌与两个历史节点并排⽐较，最终倾向于 1998 年模板。
⽀持 1998 年类⽐的理由在于：当年俄罗斯违约与⻓期资本管理公司（ LTCM ）危机是外部冲击，
但美国经济增⻓本⾝保持完好；⼀旦流动性恢复、美联储转向宽松，市场迅速收复失地。对应
到当下， AI 增⻓逻辑可能同样完好，市场重定价的是建设融资与推进节奏。
此外，当前科技领导者拥有真实收⼊、现⾦流与资产负债表，这与 2000 年泡沫时期的公司有本
质区别；推迟数据中⼼建设并不消灭算⼒需求，受限产能反⽽可能改善存量资产的经济性。
与 2000 年最相似的不适感在于⽀出规模的庞⼤以及回报的不确定性。核⼼问题是：市场正在清
算⼀个健全的增⻓故事，还是正在发现其经济逻辑本⾝存在缺陷？ Privorotsky 倾向于前者，但

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

强调需求、利⽤率与回报数据必须保持稳固。
AI 建设四类担忧，市场抓错了重点
⾼盛梳理了⽀撑本轮抛售的四类市场担忧：⼀是资本不⾜以⽀撑建设规模；⼆是收⼊不⾜以证
明投资合理；三是电⼒与许可容量不⾜以⽀持算⼒扩张；四是总体⽀出轨迹不可持续。
Privorotsky 认为，市场⽬前最关注的是第三条 —— 电⼒与许可问题，但这恰恰是四类担忧中最
不重要的⼀条。供给受限并不等于需求消失。真正需要回答的问题是：收⼊端能否最终证明所
投⼊资本的合理性。
在政治变量⽅⾯，得州候任州⻓ Gina Hinojosa 的表态⽐市场解读的更为温和。她明确表⽰： " 我
们可以实现经济发展，同时保护得克萨斯州⺠众，这两者并不互斥 " ，并重申 " 我并⾮憎恨数据中
⼼ " 。 Privorotsky 认为，这更像是监管层⾯的重置，⽽⾮意识形态上的拒绝，实际⽀出或将好于
当前市场折价所隐含的预期。
技术位与买点前提
从技术⾯看，纳斯达克已跌破 200 ⽇均线，标普 500 则刚好守在该均线上⽅，这⼀⽀撑位能否守
住⾄关重要。负 gamma 环境意味着市场在任⼀⽅向均存在剧烈波动的空间。
Privorotsky 在质量偏好上明确点名台积电与阿斯⻨，认为在当前不确定性下应向⾼质量科技资
产倾斜。
他同时列出改变市场叙事所需的三项条件：AI 投资形成政治正反馈、融资市场企稳，以及底层
⽀出与需求保持完好的实证证据。反证条件同样清晰：若 AI 资本开⽀遭到实质性推迟且收⼊端证
伪投资逻辑， 1998 年类⽐失效，需转向 2000 年模板；若融资市场率先出现再融资失败，下跌性
质将发⽣根本改变。
从更⻓周期看， Privorotsky 援引历史数据指出， 1950 年以来 19 次中期选举后 12 个⽉，标普 500
全部录得上涨 —— 但他强调，这是 12 个⽉维度的观察，⽽⾮⼀个⽉的预测，短期择时依然困
难。能源⽅⾯，油价单⽇反弹约 4% ，市场此前对伊朗局势快速缓和的定价过于乐观，谈判尚未
解决，能源冲击对利率、增⻓预期与⻛险偏好的复杂化影响仍是稳定化进程的重要变量。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 287: Apollo拟豪掷750亿美元采购AI芯片，规模远超任何一家Neocloud

Source PDF: all report pdf/Apollo拟豪掷750亿美元采购AI芯片，规模远超任何一家Neocloud.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

私募巨头 Apollo 正以 750 亿美元的惊⼈押注重塑 AI 芯⽚融资格局 ——350 亿美元购买⾕歌
TPU 租赁给 Anthropic ， 400 亿美元购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚租赁给 SpaceX 。这⼀规模
远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave 的 270 亿美元，使 Apollo 跻⾝仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌的
全球芯⽚储备第四极。
摘要：
私募巨头 Apollo Global Management 正以前所未有的规模押注 AI 芯⽚市场，其两笔待完成的芯
⽚交易合计⾦额⾼达 750 亿美元，不仅远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来累计 270 亿
美元的芯⽚融资规模，更是排名第⼆的竞争对⼿的逾 10 倍。
据 The Information 最新报道，SpaceX 宣布将租⽤价值 400 亿美元的英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，⽽
Apollo 将通过旗下⼦公司购⼊这批芯⽚后再租赁给 SpaceX 。此前， Apollo 已于今年 6 ⽉宣布联

合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，为其购买⾕歌张量处理器（ TPU ）并租赁给 Anthropic 的交易安排了
350 亿美元融资。
⼀旦上述交易完成，在 AI 芯⽚持有规模上，Apollo 将仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌等超⼤规模云
服务商，成为全球芯⽚储备最雄厚的⾮超⼤规模玩家，彻底改变 AI 基础设施的竞争格局。 Apollo
在这些交易中通过撮合交易及管理融资基⾦赚取费⽤。
两笔巨型交易奠定芯⽚霸主地位
Apollo 当前的芯⽚布局由两笔核⼼交易构成。
其⼀是今年 6 ⽉宣布的 350 亿美元交易：Apollo 联合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，购买⾕歌 TPU 并租
赁给 AI 初创公司 Anthropic 。其⼆是上周随 SpaceX 公告浮出⽔⾯的 400 亿美元交易：Apollo 通过
⼦公司购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，再租赁给⻢斯克旗下的 SpaceX 。
两笔交易合计 750 亿美元，使 Apollo 在芯⽚融资领域的体量远超任何⼀家 neocloud 。据 The

Information 汇编的数据， CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来通过芯⽚抵押融资累计筹资 270 亿美元，位
居 neocloud 之⾸；其后依次为 Valor （ 54 亿美元）、 Nscale （ 49 亿美元）、 IREN （ 39 亿美元）、
Lambda （ 15 亿美元）、 Crusoe （ 11 亿美元）、 Nebius （ 7.75 亿美元）及 Sharon AI （ 3.56 亿美
元）。
值得注意的是，上述统计仅涵盖以 GPU 和 TPU 为抵押的融资，不包括各家 neocloud 为运营融资
⽽发⾏的可转换债券及信贷额度。此外，上周另有⼀笔由花旗集团、美国银⾏与 Blackstone 联合
安排、规模达 630 亿美元的交易 —— ⽤于为 Anthropic 采购 Broadcom 芯⽚提供融资 —— 亦未纳⼊
上述⽐较，原因是各⽅未披露这批芯⽚的实际持有⽅。
芯⽚融资模式： SPV 结构的规模化复制
Apollo 所采⽤的芯⽚融资结构，脱胎于 CoreWeave 与 Blackstone 在 2023 年共同开创的特殊⽬的

载体（ SPV ）模式。在这⼀安排下， neocloud 与其⾦融合作⽅共同设⽴ SPV ，由 SPV 发⾏债务
以购买芯⽚，芯⽚终端⽤⼾⽀付的租⾦则⽤于偿还债务。
Apollo 的操作逻辑与此⼀脉相承，但规模更为庞⼤。与许多 neocloud 不同的是， Apollo 并未额外
投⼊资本建设⽤于存放芯⽚的数据中⼼，其商业模式更为聚焦 —— 通过购买芯⽚、出租给终端
⽤⼾，赚取交易撮合费⽤及基⾦管理费。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

超⼤规模云服务商则⽆需借助 SPV 结构，可直接通过⾃由现⾦流、股权发⾏或传统公司债完成
芯⽚采购，这也是 Apollo 与超⼤规模云服务商之间的本质区别。
保险资本驱动 Apollo 转型
Apollo 的芯⽚豪赌，折射出这家⽼牌私募机构过去⼗年的深刻转型。 Apollo 由亿万富翁 Leon
Black 联合创⽴，成⽴已逾 36 年，早年以杠杆收购闻名，其中包括 2008 年斥资近 300 亿美元收购
赌场巨头哈拉斯娱乐。
过去⼗年间， Apollo ⼤规模吸纳来⾃保险公司的资本。保险资⾦偏好收益率⾼于投资级债券、
但信⽤评级仍维持投资级的资产类别，⽽芯⽚融资交易恰好契合这⼀需求。这⼀资本结构的转
变，为 Apollo 在 AI 基础设施领域的⼤规模布局提供了充裕的弹药。

芯⽚融资对 Apollo ⽽⾔是⼀个全新的业务⽅向，但其背后的逻辑与传统私募信贷如出⼀辙：以资
产为抵押，提供结构化融资，赚取稳定的利差收益。随着 AI 算⼒需求持续膨胀， Apollo 正将这⼀
逻辑推向新的量级。

## Document 376: MiniMax创始人罕见公开访谈：将一直开源直到实现AGI，盈利靠压低推理成本不靠涨价

Source PDF: all report pdf/MiniMax创始人罕见公开访谈：将一直开源直到实现AGI，盈利靠压低推理成本不靠涨价.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 29
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

MiniMax 创始⼈闫俊杰⾸次英⽂播客亮相，抛出惊⼈论断： AI 盈利靠降成本⽽⾮涨价，低
推理成本既是收⼊护城河，也能放⼤模型后训练规模。截⾄ 8 ⽉，公司 ARR 已超 8 亿美元，
B2B 占⽐逾 80% 。他还宣布将坚持开源直⾄ AGI 实现 —— 因为 " 智能应属于所有⼈ " 。
摘要：
中国 AI 公司正在尝试⾛出⼀条与硅⾕不同的道路。 MiniMax 创始⼈、董事⻓兼 CEO 闫俊杰（ IO ）
在⾸次英⽂播客访谈中表⽰，公司将坚持开源，直到实现通⽤⼈⼯智能（ AGI ）；⽽实现盈利的
关键，不是提⾼模型价格，⽽是通过技术创新持续压低推理成本。
在他看来，美国 AI ⽣态依靠巨额资本和算⼒资源推动模型能⼒突破，中国公司则必须在有限的算
⼒条件下提⾼效率。 MiniMax 希望证明，即使⽆法获得同等规模的顶级芯⽚，也可以通过模型架
构、基础设施和算法优化，缩⼩与美国前沿模型的差距。
这套思路已经开始反映在商业模式上。闫俊杰透露，截⾄ 8 ⽉， MiniMax 的年度经常性收⼊年化

运⾏率（ ARR ）已超过 8 亿美元，其中企业业务占⽐超过 80% 。他认为，低推理成本不仅可以让
客⼾以更低价格使⽤ AI ，还能让公司以更低成本进⾏强化学习和后训练，从⽽形成商业增⻓与
模型能⼒提升相互促进的循环。
与此同时， MiniMax 正在推进语⾔模型与视频⽣成模型的融合，希望让 AI 从⽣成⽂本和视频⽚
段，进⼀步发展为能够规划、执⾏并完成复杂项⽬的智能体。闫俊杰强调， AGI 不应该属于某⼀
家企业、某⼀个国家或某个组织，⽽应该属于所有⼈。
华尔街⻅闻整理要点如下：
1 、 MiniMax 将坚持开源，直到 AGI 实现。闫俊杰认为，开源不是商业变现的替代⽅案，⽽是推
动商业化和技术创新的催化剂。开发者可以基于模型开发新产品，形成反馈循环，帮助模型更快
迭代。
2 、低推理成本是 MiniMax 商业模式的核⼼护城河。闫俊杰认为， AI 盈利不应该依赖涨价，⽽应
该通过技术创新降低推理成本。成本下降既能吸引更多客⼾，也能让公司以更低成本扩⼤后训练
规模。
3 、中国和美国的 AI ⽣态正在形成不同的发展路径。美国依靠资本、芯⽚和算⼒资源推动前沿模

型发展，中国公司则更重视单位算⼒的使⽤效率。资源约束反⽽促使中国 AI 实验室在架构、基础
设施和算法上持续创新。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

从资源约束到效率优势： MiniMax 选择了⼀条不同的 AI 道路
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，资源约束并⾮⼀个可以轻易消除的短期问题，⽽是从创⽴之初就必须⾯对的
现实。
美国头部 AI 公司拥有更充⾜的资本、芯⽚和算⼒资源，可以通过⼤量实验寻找最优技术路线。中
国公司则必须更加谨慎地使⽤每⼀单位算⼒。这种差异逐渐塑造了 MiniMax 的组织⽂化和技术战
略。
闫俊杰将公司的⽬标概括为：最⼤限度地降低推理成本，同时让每个⼈都能够享受到前沿智
能。
他认为， AI ⾏业最终的竞争不只是模型性能的竞争，也是单位算⼒能够创造多少智能的竞争。
开源不是放弃商业化，⽽是建⽴更⼤的创新⽹络
在中美 AI 公司的发展路径中，开源与闭源已经成为⼀个重要分⽔岭。
OpenAI 、 Anthropic 等美国公司主要采取闭源模式，通过控制模型访问权限和 API 服务实现商业
化。中国 AI 公司则更加积极地采⽤开放权重模式，允许开发者下载模型，并在⾃⼰的硬件上部署
和修改。
MiniMax 选择站在后者⼀边。闫俊杰表⽰，开源并不是商业变现的替代⽅案，反⽽是推动商业

化和进⼀步创新的催化剂。
他以公司 H3 模型为例。按照他的介绍， H3 发布后的前两周，已经有⼤约 300 个基于该模型构建
的开放模型出现。
其中，⼀家美国公司 fal.ai 基于 H3 进⾏后训练，开发出 H3 Max ，能够在 3 秒内⽣成 5 秒的视频，
实现接近实时的视频⽣成。
这意味着，开源模型的价值并不局限于原始模型本⾝。外部开发者可以在其基础上进⾏优化，
开发出原本没有被设想过的应⽤。
闫俊杰举例称，开发者已经利⽤相关技术搭建了 24 ⼩时运⾏的 AI 直播系统，内容可以根据⽤⼾
需求实时⽣成。类似的能⼒还可能延伸⾄实时游戏、教育和其他交互式体验。
4 、 MiniMax 正在押注语⾔模型与视频模型的融合。公司⽬前拥有 M 系列语⾔模型和 H 系列多模态
模型，未来希望将两者整合为统⼀系统，让 AI 从⽣成内容升级为完成⼴告、影视、设计等复杂⼯
作。
5 、 AI Agent 的下⼀阶段不只是执⾏任务，⽽是⾃主设定⽬标并验证结果。闫俊杰认为，真正有
价值的智能体应当具备⻓期任务中的⾃主性。

6 、 MiniMax 的竞争优势不仅在于技术，也在于创始⼈的资本配置能⼒。 Scottish Mortgage 投资
经理 Lawrence Burns 认为，闫俊杰能够减少⽆效实验、作出关键技术选择，并以更少的资源取
得较好的研发成果。
7 、盈利仍⾯临价格竞争和前沿研发成本上升两⼤挑战。 Lawrence Burns 指出，中国 AI 市场竞争
激烈， MiniMax 仍需持续投⼊以保持技术前沿，市场空间虽然巨⼤，但盈利能⼒和利润率仍需验
证。

### Page 3

对于企业和政府客⼾，开源还有另⼀个优势：模型可以部署在⾃⼰的硬件上，从⽽更好地保护
数据和隐私。因此， MiniMax 希望通过开放模型权重，让开发者、⽤⼾和模型公司形成⼀个相互
促进的⽣态。
闫俊杰甚⾄明确表⽰，公司计划⼀直开源，直到 AGI 实现。
这并不意味着开源模式没有商业化问题。模型下载量和开发者数量最终仍需要转化为 API 收⼊、
企业客⼾和实际使⽤量。但 MiniMax 的判断是，开放⽣态能够扩⼤模型的应⽤范围，并让公司以
更低成本获得更多技术反馈。
盈利不靠涨价：低推理成本同时决定收⼊与研发效率
相⽐开源战略，闫俊杰对 MiniMax 商业模式的解释更加值得关注。
⽬前， AI ⾏业普遍⾯临较⾼的研发和算⼒投⼊，很多公司仍处于亏损状态。对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，
如何在保持模型能⼒增⻓的同时实现盈利，是投资者最关⼼的问题之⼀。
闫俊杰的答案是：AI 的利润不是通过提⾼价格获得的，⽽是通过创新降低推理成本获得的。
他认为，低推理成本同时构成公司的收⼊护城河和利润护城河。
⼀⽅⾯，推理成本下降意味着 MiniMax 可以⽤更低价格向客⼾提供服务，扩⼤市场覆盖范围。并

不是所有企业都需要全球最先进的模型，很多⽇常办公、编程和业务流程，只需要性能⾜够
好、价格合理的模型。
如果模型性能达到业务需求，⽽价格明显低于竞争对⼿，企业采⽤ AI 的投资回报率就会提⾼。
另⼀⽅⾯，低推理成本还会影响模型本⾝的研发效率。
闫俊杰特别强调了后训练的重要性。在模型完成初始训练之后，还需要通过监督微调、强化学
习和数据合成等⽅式，让模型不断练习、纠正错误并提⾼复杂任务处理能⼒。
这些过程都需要⼤量推理计算。因此，推理成本越低，公司就越有能⼒扩⼤后训练规模，让模
型获得更多练习机会。
Lawrence Burns 对此给出了⼀个⾼尔夫球的⽐喻：两名球⼿都需要不断练习才能进步，但如果
其中⼀⼈购买练习球的成本更低，那么在相同预算下，他就能进⾏更多练习，最终更有可能成
为更好的球⼿。
MiniMax 的优势就在于拥有更便宜的 “ 练习球 ” 。这意味着，低成本并不只是⼀个定价优势，还可
能转化为模型能⼒提升的优势。
闫俊杰透露，截⾄ 8 ⽉， MiniMax 的 ARR 已经超过 8 亿美元，其中 B2B 业务占⽐超过 80% 。

不过， ARR 是按照当前收⼊⽔平年化计算的运⾏率，并不等于已经实现的全年收⼊。
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，理想的发展路径是：推理成本持续下降，吸引更多客⼾使⽤，收⼊规模扩
⼤，再将资源投⼊后训练和模型研发，最终推动收⼊增⻓快于成本增⻓。这也是公司希望建⽴
的增⻓⻜轮。

### Page 4

中国 AI 公司的另⼀种竞争⽅式：单位算⼒创造更多智能
在谈及中美 AI 产业竞争时，闫俊杰认为，两种⽣态系统正在形成不同的发展模式。
美国 AI 公司拥有更多资本、算⼒和芯⽚资源，能够通过⼤规模计算推动模型能⼒突破。中国公司
则需要更加重视每单位算⼒的效率。
他表⽰，美国的 AI ⽣态正在建⽴由巨额资本⽀撑的封闭系统，⽽中国的 AI ⽣态则正在建⽴由效率
驱动的协作体系。
他并不认为两种⽣态最终会⾛向完全相同的道路。这种差异不仅体现在商业模式上，也体现在
技术架构和研发⽅法上。
MiniMax 重点投⼊三个领域：模型架构、基础设施和优化技术。闫俊杰表⽰，公司从⼀开始就
希望模型能够在不同芯⽚上保持良好的运⾏表现，⽽不是完全依赖某⼀种硬件。
在他看来，算⼒越受限制，算法和推理效率的重要性就越⾼。
这种资源约束反⽽让公司的⼯程师和研究⼈员变得更加优秀。这也解释了为什么 MiniMax 将企业
⽂化概括为扁平、专注和节俭。
公司⽬前只有约 500 名员⼯，因此必须把资源集中在最重要的事情上。

闫俊杰提出了两项研发原则：第⼀，让事情尽可能简单；第⼆，始终建设基础设施。他认为，
直接推出产品可能更快，但⻓期来看，基础设施才是实现规模化发展的基础。
Scottish Mortgage 投资经理 Lawrence Burns 则认为，闫俊杰的⼀个突出优势，是他能够同时扮
演优秀科学家和资本配置者的⻆⾊。
在很多美国 AI 实验室中，研究⼈员可以利⽤充⾜的资本和芯⽚资源开展⼤量实验。但 MiniMax 没
有这样的条件，因此必须减少⽆效尝试，尽早判断哪些技术路线值得投⼊。
Burns 认为，闫俊杰能够更准确地判断技术⽅向，并在较少的实验选择中取得较好的结果。这不
仅提⾼了资本效率，也有助于吸引和留住中国顶尖 AI ⼈才。
从视频⽣成到完整任务：语⾔模型与视频模型的融合
MiniMax 并不打算只做语⾔模型或者视频⽣成模型。公司⽬前拥有两个主要模型系列： M 系列语
⾔模型，以及 H 系列多模态模型。
M 系列主要⾯向编程、智能体和⽣产⼒应⽤； H 系列则专注于视频⽣成，并能够处理图像和⾳

频。
闫俊杰表⽰，公司希望在未来将两个系列整合为⼀个统⼀模型。
这⼀战略背后，是他对 AI 应⽤形态变化的判断。
⽬前的视频⽣成模型能够根据提⽰词⽣成视频⽚段，但在理解复杂指令、根据反馈修改内容以
及完成连续任务⽅⾯仍有不⾜。

### Page 5

例如，在⼴告、电影和设计⼯作中，⽤⼾不仅需要⽣成⼀段视频，还需要不断调整镜头、修改
内容、理解创作意图，并最终完成⼀个完整项⽬。
这就需要语⾔模型的理解、规划和执⾏能⼒与视频模型的⽣成能⼒结合起来。 Lawrence Burns
认为，这种融合可能打开⽐单纯⽣成视频⽚段更⼤的市场。
当 AI 能够从⽣成内容升级为完成整个项⽬时，企业愿意⽀付的费⽤也可能显著提⾼。
与此同时， MiniMax 还在押注 AI Agent 。闫俊杰认为，下⼀代智能体的竞争重点，不只是能否完
成复杂任务，⽽是能否⾃主制定⽬标，并验证任务结果。
⽬前， MiniMax 已经将编程和 Agent 能⼒作为 M 系列模型的核⼼⽅向。他还特别提到，⾃⼰当时
正集中精⼒研发下⼀代模型 M3 Pro ，并认为它有望成为⼀项重要突破。
盈利仍⾯临两⼤挑战：价格战与前沿研发成本
尽管 MiniMax 已经展现出较快的收⼊增⻓，但盈利仍然是公司必须⾯对的问题。
Lawrence Burns 指出，今年上半年， MiniMax 收⼊接近增⻓四倍，但报告亏损规模也达到原来

的约三倍。
他认为，公司实现盈利⾯临两项主要挑战。
第⼀，中国 AI 市场的价格竞争⾮常激烈。
MiniMax 需要与 DeepSeek 等竞争对⼿争夺客⼾，⽽激烈的价格竞争可能压低整个⾏业的服务价
格。
第⼆，维持前沿模型能⼒的成本仍在不断上升。
随着 AI 模型不断进步，训练和推理所需的资源也在增加。这也是 MiniMax 在 7 ⽉份融资 20 亿美元
的原因之⼀。
在 Burns 看来，公司需要依靠精简的成本结构、正确的技术选择，以及能够⼤规模变现的市场来
应对这些挑战。
但他也指出，⽬前已经出现了⼀些积极变化：研发⽀出的增⻓速度开始低于销售收⼊增⻓速
度，企业业务收⼊也在快速扩⼤。
按照他的介绍，企业业务收⼊增⻓约⼋倍，⽬前占总收⼊的 63% 。⽽闫俊杰提到的超过 80% ，
则是按年化⼝径计算的业务占⽐。
企业客⼾的增加有助于改善商业化能⼒，因为随着模型性能提升，企业可以利⽤ AI 完成更多⼯
作，也更愿意为更⾼的⽣产⼒⽀付费⽤。
不过， Burns 强调， MiniMax 仍然是⼀项早期阶段的投资，最终结果存在较⼤的不确定性。这也

是基⾦控制其投资组合持仓规模的原因之⼀。
AGI 不属于任何⼀家企业： MiniMax 的最终愿景

### Page 6

访谈最后， Lawrence Burns 询问闫俊杰，如果 MiniMax 实现⾃⼰的⽬标，世界将会发⽣怎样的
改变。
闫俊杰给出的回答是：真正的问题不是 AGI 能否实现，⽽是 AGI 最终将让谁受益。
在他看来， AGI 不应该属于某⼀家企业、某⼀个国家或者某个组织，⽽应该属于所有⼈。
他认为，使⽤模型的⼈、基于模型开发应⽤的开发者，以及为模型作出贡献的研究⼈员，都应
该成为 AGI ⽣态的⼀部分。
这也是 MiniMax 将公司⼝号定为 “Intelligence with everyone” （与所有⼈共享智能），⽽不
是 “Intelligence for everyone” （为所有⼈提供智能）的原因。
两者看似只有⼀个词的区别，背后的含义却不同。
前者强调智能不仅应该服务于所有⼈，更应该由所有⼈共同参与和拥有。
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，开源不仅是⼀种技术策略，也是⼀种关于 AI 未来归属的选择。
⽽对于投资者来说，最终需要验证的仍然是另⼀件事：这种开放和低成本的路线，能否在激烈
的⾏业竞争中形成可持续的商业回报。

以下为访谈原⽂，由 AI 辅助翻译：
这场访谈由英国投资信托 Scottish Mortgage 旗下播客《 Invest in Progress 》推出，也是

种由效率驱动的协作体系。

⾸先，并不是所有⼯作都需要使⽤最顶尖的模型。

MiniMax 是⼀家业务覆盖全球、专注于⼈⼯智能的公司。它与我们持有的另⼀家投资组合公司
Anthropic 有⼀些共同之处。
但这家中国企业与美国同⾏有着截然不同的特点。
MiniMax 的⾸席执⾏官是闫俊杰博⼠，也被称为  IO 。
正如接下来⼤家将听到的，他奉⾏的理念是：以尽可能低的成本提供智能。
⽬前， MiniMax 既⽆法获得⼤量最先进的计算芯⽚，也没有⾜够的预算来购买这些芯⽚。因
此，它不得不学会⽤更少的资源做更多的事情。

⽣成能⼒。

### Page 8

此外， MiniMax 还是开源模式的积极倡导者。
Anthropic 选择将模型保持封闭，并向客⼾收取使⽤费⽤；⽽  MiniMax 则允许⽤⼾下载其技术，
并在⾃⼰的硬件上运⾏。
这意味着，⽤⼾实际上可以免费使⽤  MiniMax 的模型，甚⾄可以根据⾃⼰的需求进⾏修改。
乍⼀看，这似乎不是⼀种特别好的商业模式。但实际上，这种⽅式能够扩⼤⽤⼾群体，同时帮助
公司以更低的成本、更快的速度进⾏创新和改进产品。
归根结底，⼈⼯智能竞赛并不只发⽣在硅⾕。
MiniMax 为  Scottish Mortgage 提供了⼀个投资机会，让我们能够参与⼀家采⽤不同发展路径的
竞争者。
这是  IO ⾸次接受英⽂播客采访。提前提醒⼀下，他会使⽤⼀些专业术语。如果你对推理

### Page 9

这也是我们为什么选择  MiniMax 这个名字。
Mini ，意味着尽可能降低推理成本； Max ，则意味着尽可能提⾼智能⽔平。
我们的最终⽬标，就是让智能带来的价值能够惠及所有⼈。

MiniMax 的产品布局：语⾔模型与多模态模型
Lawrence Burns ：
对于那些还不熟悉  MiniMax 产品的听众，能否简单介绍⼀下公司的主要产品？它们分别⾯向消

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我们有⼀些不同的产品，但⽬前已经将它们归纳为两个模型系列。
第⼀个是  M 系列模型，也就是语⾔模型。

它主要⾯向智能应⽤和⽣产⼒场景，例如编程以及  AI Agent （智能体）。
第⼆个是  H 系列模型，是视频⽣成模型。
它能够同时处理视频、图像和⾳频。
⽬前，我们最具代表性的  H 系列模型是  H3 。
它采⽤开源模式，同时通过  API 提供服务，也开放模型权重供⽤⼾下载，还可以通过  MiniMax
⾃⼰设计的产品使⽤。
事实上，我认为在过去⼀个⽉⾥， H3 已经成为全球下载量最⾼的开放权重模型。
⼀个⽉内，它的下载量可能达到了 2500 万次。

### Page 10

更重要的是，我们并不认为这两个模型系列未来会永远彼此独⽴。

我认为，这有可能在未来⼏个⽉内实现。
Lawrence Burns ：

你成⻓于中国河南省的⼀个⼩城镇。河南是中国重要的农业省份之⼀。你的⽗亲是⼀名当地学校

闫俊杰（ IO ）：

河南这个名字，意思就是⻩河以南。⻩河是中国的⺟亲河。
我⼩时候，家⾥⼀直有很多书。

到了初中阶段，我想⾃⼰已经开始学习⼀些⼤学⽔平的数学和计算机科学知识了。

读了很多关于数学和计算机科学的书之后，真正让我感兴趣的是数学与计算机科学的结合。
因为这种结合使我能够利⽤数学和算法，通过编程解决现实世界中的问题。
这也很⾃然地让我⾛向了机器学习领域。
那是在⼤约 20 年前，当时机器学习仍然是⼀个相当独特的研究⽅向。
因此，在完成本科学习之后，我决定继续攻读机器学习⽅向的博⼠学位。
因为我相信，如果能够把这些⽅法的规模不断扩⼤， AI 最终就会变得更加强⼤，也更加实⽤。

ChatGPT 发布，改变了  MiniMax 对  AI 发展速度的判断
Lawrence Burns ：
你在 2021 年 12 ⽉创⽴了  MiniMax 。
刚才你谈到了通⽤智能和⾯向消费者的产品。公司最早推出的消费级产品是  Glow ，这是⼀个允
许⽤⼾与虚拟陪伴⻆⾊聊天的平台，⽽且很快就积累了数百万⽤⼾。
就在  Glow 发布之后不久， OpenAI 推出了  ChatGPT 。
这显然也彻底改变了消费级  AI 市场。
ChatGPT 的出现，应该进⼀步验证了你的判断：市场对这项技术存在着巨⼤的潜在需求。
不过，我很好奇， ChatGPT 的发布究竟如何改变了你对  AI 发展⽅向的认识？它是否也改变了你

对  MiniMax 未来定位的思考？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：

正如你刚才所说，在  MiniMax 成⽴的第⼀年，我们只知道  AGI ⾮常重要，但当时并没有明确的
时间表。
所以在第⼀年，我们主要做了⼏件事情：
⾸先，搭建公司的基础设施。
其次，训练最初版本的语⾔模型，以及图像和⾳频⽣成模型。
同时，我们还希望开发⼀款应⽤，⽤来测试和验证这些模型的实际性能。
这就是我们推出  Glow 的原因。
当时，我们并没有预料到它会吸引这么多⽤⼾。
我们最初只是想做⼀次实验，所以才开发了  Glow 。

### Page 13

⼤约四个⽉之后， ChatGPT 发布了。
我认为， ChatGPT 改变了我们对  AI 发展时间表的判断。
它让我们意识到，模型规模化发展的速度和幅度，都必须⽐原先预期的⼤得多、快得多。
因此，我们开始扩⼤基础设施规模，扩⼤数据规模，也扩⼤模型本⾝的规模。
Lawrence Burns ：
所以，它不仅激发了你们的雄⼼，也让你们意识到，⾃⼰正在从事的事业可能具有多么巨⼤的发
展空间。
那么， ChatGPT 的出现是否成为推动  MiniMax 加速发展的催化剂？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
是的，是的。
实际上，在  ChatGPT 发布之前，中国只有⼀家公司 ——MiniMax—— 在研究⼤语⾔模型。
⽽  ChatGPT 发布之后，很快就有⼤约 20 家公司开始在中国研发⼤语⾔模型。
因此，市场迅速变得竞争激烈。
为什么  MiniMax 从⼀开始就坚持多模态路线？
Lawrence Burns ：
从很早开始，你就把多模态放在了公司战略的核⼼位置。
换句话说，你们希望模型不仅能够⽣成⽂本，还能够⽣成图像、视频、⾳乐和语⾳。

如果联系到  ChatGPT 的发展路径来看， OpenAI 显然已经不再像过去那样全⾯推进多模态战
略。例如，他们已经放弃了视频⽣成⽅向。
但与此同时，坚持多模态路线也意味着你们需要投⼊更多资源。
能否解释⼀下，为什么你认为多模态是正确的发展道路，⽽不是像其他公司那样，先集中⼒量做
好⼀两个⽅向？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
真正的原因是，在  MiniMax 创⽴之初，我们并不知道  AI 最终会以什么具体形态发展。
所以，我们选择同时扩⼤不同⽅向的技术能⼒，并希望在技术积累⾜够之后，将它们整合起来。
我认为，多模态能够丰富训练数据的多样性，也是未来扩⼤模型能⼒的必要路径。
当然，这也给我们带来了更多挑战。
如果⼀开始只专注于某⼀种模态，我们可能会取得更快的初期进展。

### Page 14

但是，多模态路线也正是我们区别于其他公司的地⽅。
AI Agent ：下⼀阶段的竞争，不只是完成任务，⽽是⾃主设定⽬标
Lawrence Burns ：
你曾经表⽰， AI Agent （⼈⼯智能智能体）将成为公司未来的重要增⻓驱动⼒。
你们把⾃⼰的智能体产品命名为  Mavis ，并希望⽤⼾将它理解为⼀位  AI 管家，能够规划并完成

闫俊杰（ IO ）：

这意味着，它们需要在⻓期任务中具备真正的⾃主性。

MiniMax 如何实现盈利？低推理成本才是真正的护城河
Lawrence Burns ：
和很多  AI 公司⼀样，你们⽬前仍然处于亏损状态。
⽆论是对  MiniMax ，还是对整个⾏业，我们都能够接受这⼀点，前提是能够看到增⻓⻜轮真正
运转起来。
也就是说，随着产品能⼒不断提升，使⽤产品的⼈越来越多，收⼊也随之增加，⽽这些收⼊⼜能
够重新投⼊研发，推动下⼀轮技术进步，最终实现盈利。
这是否也是你看待  MiniMax 商业模式的⽅式？
你⼜凭什么相信，公司⻓期来看能够实现盈利？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为， AI ⾏业的盈利并不是通过提⾼价格实现的，⽽是通过创新不断降低推理成本实现的。

### Page 15

我认为，低推理成本是我们在收⼊和利润⽅⾯的护城河。
这种效率使我们能够建⽴⼀种⾼度可持续的商业模式，其核⼼是  API 服务，⽽不是⼀次性的交
易。
举个例⼦，今年以来，我们的  ARR 增⻓⾮常出⾊。
截⾄ 8 ⽉份，公司的  ARR 已经超过 8 亿美元，其中  B2B （企业对企业）业务占⽐超过 80% 。
我认为，随着模型性能不断提升，同时我们继续保持价格竞争⼒，公司的收⼊增⻓速度⼀定会超
过成本增⻓速度。
这就是我们理想中的发展路径。
Lawrence Burns ：
你刚才提到了  ARR ，也就是年度经常性收⼊的年化运⾏率。
正如你所说， 8 ⽉份这⼀数字已经达到 8 亿美元。
为了让听众更好地理解这个指标，可以这样解释：如果按照 8 ⽉份的收⼊⽔平，将其乘以 12 ，就
可以得到约 8 亿美元的年化收⼊运⾏率。
我想回到去年 12 ⽉你告诉我的⼀个判断。
当时，你认为最好的中国  AI 模型与美国顶尖模型相⽐，性能⼤约落后 5% ，但成本却只有后者的
⼗分之⼀。
从那时到现在，双⽅在模型能⼒和价格⽅⾯的差距发⽣了怎样的变化？这⼜意味着什么？

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，真正重要的并不是某⼀个时间点双⽅之间存在多⼤的差距，⽽是双⽅能⼒提升的速度。
因为⽆论是美国模型还是中国模型，⽬前都还处于通往  AGI 的⾮常早期阶段。
⾸先，我想说，最后那 5% 的差距，实际上可能⽐最初达到 95% 要困难得多。
所以，我认为，⽬前剩下的这部分差距，可能还需要我花费⼀些时间才能解决。
但因为我们仍然处于  AGI 发展的早期阶段，所以真正重要的是我们能够以多快的速度进步。
对于我们⽽⾔，尤其是对于⼀家中国  AI 公司来说，算⼒始终是⼀种约束。
因此，从第⼀天开始，我们就知道必须尽可能提⾼推理效率。
事实上，在过去三年⾥，我们的技术进步速度已经⼤致达到了可⽐的⽔平。
我们关注的是进步速度，⽽不是某⼀个时间点的排名。
这也促使我们在模型架构和基础设施⽅⾯不断创新，⽽不仅仅依赖芯⽚本⾝。

### Page 16

我们相信，如果能够更加⾼效地使⽤算⼒，就能够开展更多的后训练⼯作，进⼀步提升模型能

Lawrence Burns ：

AI 不⼀定越强越好：真正的市场可能在性价⽐
Lawrence Burns ：
当我们思考  AI 市场机会时，我认为，你们如此重视每单位智能的价格，背后还有⼀个重要逻

辑。
随着技术前沿不断向前推进，整个  AI 能⼒体系也在不断扩展。
但实际上，我们并不需要全球最顶尖的前沿模型来处理各种普通消费者的问题，或者完成⾮常基

对于⼤量  AI 使⽤场景⽽⾔，重要的并不是拥有世界上最复杂、最强⼤的模型，⽽是拥有⼀个性
能⾜够好的模型，同时价格要合理，让企业采⽤它能够获得正向的投资回报率（ ROI ）。
⽽这实际上可能构成⼀个⾮常庞⼤的市场。
当然，也会存在⼀些需要顶尖模型的场景。例如前沿科学研究、癌症治疗，以及纽约、⾹港等地

你是否也是这样看待整个市场机会的？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
实际上，除了价格之外，还有另外⼀个重要因素。

### Page 17

更低的推理成本意味着，我们能够更加⾼效地扩⼤后训练规模。
因为在后训练过程中，⽆论是强化学习，还是数据合成，都依赖于推理。
所以，更⾼效的推理意味着能够开展规模更⼤的后训练。

开源与闭源：为什么  MiniMax 选择开放模型？
Lawrence Burns ：
美国和中国  AI ⽣态之间的另⼀个重⼤区别，是闭源模型与开源模型的使⽤⽅式。
OpenAI 、 Anthropic 等公司采⽤的是闭源模式，⽽开源模型在中国则更加普遍。
简单来说，采⽤开放模型的  AI 公司允许其他⼈下载并在第三⽅设备上运⾏⾃⼰的模型，⽽不需
要⽀付许可证费⽤。
此外，开放模型还允许⽤⼾进⾏更多微调，根据额外的训练数据调整模型权重。
那么，你如何看待开源模型和闭源模型各⾃的优势与劣势？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，开源并不是商业变现的替代⽅案。
恰恰相反，开源是推动商业变现和进⼀步创新的催化剂。

例如，我们刚才提到的  MiniMax H3 模型。
实际上，在它发布后的前两周，就已经有⼤约 300 个基于  H3 构建的开放模型出现。
我认为，这是现阶段能够为我们的模型提供反馈、推动进⼀步创新的最佳反馈循环。
再举⼀个例⼦。
在这 300 个基于  H3 构建的开放模型中，有⼀个⽐较知名的模型叫  H3 Max ，它来⾃⼀家美国公
司  fal.ai 。
这家公司以  H3 为基础进⾏了后训练，⽽且实现了更快的⽣成速度。
它能够在 3 秒内⽣成 5 秒钟的视频。
也就是说，它已经成为⼀个实时视频⽣成器。
实际上，这种速度将带来全新的视听内容使⽤⽅式。

### Page 18

过去根本⽆法实现的事情，现在开始变得可⾏。
例如，开发者已经利⽤这项技术搭建了 24 ⼩时不间断运⾏的  AI 直播。
直播内容可以根据⽤⼾希望看到的东西实时⽣成。
同样的能⼒还可能让实时游戏、教育以及许多其他交互式体验成为现实。
第⼆个优势在于，当客⼾能够在⾃⼰的设备上部署开放权重模型时，他们就能够保护⾃⼰的数据
和隐私。
这对于企业和政府尤其重要。
所以，我认为，开源对于模型公司、⽤⼾和开发者⽽⾔，是⼀种三⽅共赢的模式。
因此，我们计划⼀直保持模型开源，直到  AGI 实现。
Lawrence Burns ：
谢谢，这⾮常有帮助，也很有意思。
过去六个⽉⾥，我们已经看到， AI 主权，或者说对  AI 模型的⾃主控制权，正在变得越来越重
要。
⼈们开始担⼼：如果⾃⼰使⽤的是⼀个由中⼼化机构控制的模型，那么当⾃⼰的业务建⽴在这个
模型之上之后，未来还能否持续获得访问权限？
另外，⾃⼰的数据究竟会发⽣什么？
这些担忧确实正在出现，也正如你所说，这给开放模型带来了优势。
MiniMax 的企业⽂化：扁平、专注、节俭

Lawrence Burns ：
接下来，我们谈谈  MiniMax 的企业⽂化。
我认为，可以⽤三个词来描述  MiniMax ：扁平、专注、节俭。
你们在组织架构上进⾏了相应设计，使公司能够快速应对  AI 领域发⽣的各种技术变化。
你们更倾向于内部培养⼈才，⽽不是花费极⾼的薪酬从其他公司挖⼈。
正如我们前⾯讨论的，你们也更偏好成本⾼效的解决⽅案，⽽不是依赖蛮⼒式的算⼒投⼊。

⼜是如何让公司的组织⽂化与这些约束相适应的？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
这是⼀个⾮常重要的问题。

### Page 19

我认为，我们的企业⽂化确实来⾃算⼒和资本⽅⾯的约束。
实际上，我们公司只有 500 名员⼯，所以必须专注于真正重要的事情。
在研发⽅⾯，我们有两项核⼼原则。
第⼀，让事情尽可能简单。
尽管我们拥有不同的模型，但我们的模型架构和基础设施都尽可能保持简单。
第⼆，我认为这⼀点也很重要：我们始终坚持建设基础设施。
有时候，建设基础设施的速度可能⽐直接推出产品要慢。
但我认为，这是公司未来实现规模化发展的基础。
所以，基于这两项原则 —— 尽可能简化⼀切，以及始终建设基础设施 —— 我们形成了现在的组织
⽅式。
我认为，资本效率、组织效率等其他特点，实际上都是这两项原则在资源约束下⾃然产⽣的结
果。
算⼒效率提升，会不会反⽽减少芯⽚需求？
Lawrence Burns ：
考虑到  MiniMax 已经成功降低了客⼾使⽤  AI 时的推理成本，⽽且更⼴泛地看，中国很多  AI 实

这是否会让你对未来算⼒需求，以及英伟达这类公司⽣产的芯⽚需求，产⽣不同的看法？

因为，如果全世界都能像你们⼀样⾼效地使⽤算⼒，那么市场对计算硬件的需求可能会⾮常不
同。
你认为，这是否会影响⼈们对⻓期算⼒需求⽔平的预期？
还是说，你认为这更像是杰⽂斯悖论（ Jevons Paradox ）？
也就是说，当成本下降之后，使⽤量反⽽会上升。因此，⼈们始终会希望获得尽可能多的芯⽚，
因为他们可以利⽤这些算⼒做更多事情。
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
⾸先，我们的模型架构从设计之初，就考虑到了⽆论使⽤哪种芯⽚，都应该能够保持良好的运⾏
表现。
这是我们模型设计的基本原则。
我们真正能够做的，是在三个领域保持技术领先：
模型架构；

### Page 20

基础设施；
优化技术。

⽽这种约束也让我们成为了更优秀的⼯程师和研究⼈员。
当算⼒受到限制时，算法和推理效率的重要性就会越来越⾼。
中美  AI ⽣态：两条不会汇合的发展道路
Lawrence Burns ：

种由效率驱动的协作体系。

⽽中国公司不得不提⾼每单位算⼒所能创造的价值。

开放与封闭、效率优先与资源丰富，这些差异将决定两个⽣态系统最终会创造出什么样的产品。
Lawrence Burns ：

你们⾯临着来⾃本⼟⼤型科技公司的激烈竞争。这些公司雄⼼勃勃，资⾦实⼒也⾮常雄厚。
例如字节跳动、阿⾥巴巴，以及越来越多的⽣成式  AI 创业公司。

除了我们前⾯讨论的企业⽂化因素之外，你认为听众应该如何理解  MiniMax 相对于这些竞争对

闫俊杰（ IO ）：

打造效率最⾼、能⼒最强的多模态  AGI 。

### Page 21

我们相信，效率本⾝就是智能的⼀部分。
你使⽤算⼒的效率越⾼，就能够把模型规模扩⼤到更⾼的⽔平。
开源模式也给了我们另⼀个优势。
例如，在中国，字节跳动采⽤闭源模式，⽽我们采⽤开源模式。
开发者可以基于我们的模型开发新的应⽤场景，并将这些成果反馈给我们，形成⼀个反馈循环，
帮助我们更快地改进模型。
我们还可以继续以  H3 为例。
字节跳动的  Seedance 是闭源的。
它能够⽣成⾼质量的视频，但⽣成速度相对较慢。
⽽基于  H3 构建的开源模型，则有可能实现实时视频⽣成。
这就带来了全新的东西。
从⻓远来看，我们希望把最优秀的语⾔模型和视频⽣成模型整合到同⼀个系统中。
我们认为，这将以统⼀模型的形式打开⼀个更⼤的市场机会。
这就是  MiniMax 的竞争优势。
AGI 最终应该属于谁？
Lawrence Burns ：
⾮常有意思。
最后，我想⽤我们节⽬每次采访创业者和企业时都会提出的问题来结束这次对话。
对于你⽽⾔，如果  MiniMax 实现了⾃⼰的雄⼼和愿景，世界将会变成什么样⼦？它会给世界带

来怎样的改变？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，真正的问题不是  AGI 能否实现，⽽是：
AGI 最终将让谁受益？
因为  AGI 太重要了，所以它不应该属于某⼀家企业、某⼀个国家或者某个组织。
它必须属于我们所有⼈。
这也是我⼀直努⼒⼯作的原因：实现  AGI ，确保智能能够掌握在每个⼈⼿中。
真正的问题不是  AGI 能不能实现，⽽是谁能够拥有它，以及应该通过什么⽅式实现这⼀点。

### Page 22

我认为， AGI 应该属于使⽤模型的⼈、基于模型进⾏开发的开发者，以及为模型作出贡献的研究
⼈员。
他们都是最终塑造这个模型的⼀部分。
还有⼀个更宏⼤的问题：
如果世界拥有了更多智能，我们对世界、甚⾄对整个宇宙的理解是否也会发⽣改变？
我认为，答案是肯定的。
正是这种可能性激励着我不断前进。
Lawrence Burns ：
这⾮常有帮助。
所以，对你⽽⾔，你的愿景不仅仅是追求开放权重模型，⽽是相信这种⽅式能够将  AI 的⼒量传
播给更多⼈和组织。
因为模型是开源的，他们能够在此基础上继续开发，某种程度上也就拥有了  AI 。
他们不再需要依赖另⼀家公司持续向⾃⼰提供模型。
这是不是你真正看重的东西？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
是的。
这就是为什么我们的⼝号是：
Intelligence with everyone （与所有⼈共享智能）。
⽽不是  Intelligence for everyone （为所有⼈提供智能）。
因为我认为，智能应该属于所有⼈。
Lawrence Burns ：

IO ，⾮常感谢你今天抽出时间，帮助我们了解  MiniMax 、公司的使命，以及  AI 领域正在发⽣的
各种变化。
我认为，这次对话对于我们英国以及更⼴泛的西⽅世界尤其重要。
因为我们经常把注意⼒集中在硅⾕，仿佛  AI 领域的⼀切都只发⽣在那⾥。
因此，能够听到来⾃中国的观点，以及了解中国正在开发的优秀产品和模型，对我们⾮常有帮
助。
⾮常感谢你今天分享这些⻅解。

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
⾮常感谢， Lawrence 。再⻅。
投资经理复盘： Scottish Mortgage 为什么投资  MiniMax ？
Claire Shaw ：
Lawrence ，这真是⼀场⾮常有意思的访谈。
我尤其感兴趣的是  IO 提出的⼀个观点：中国和美国的  AI ⽣态并不会沿着同⼀条道路发展。
事实上，与美国竞争对⼿相⽐， MiniMax ⽆法获得同样多的顶级计算芯⽚，但这种限制反⽽可
能成为公司⻓期竞争优势的⼀部分。
不过，按照我们的惯例，节⽬最后还是要听听你对这项投资的判断。
⾸先， 2026 年初，也就是  MiniMax 在⾹港上市时，是什么让你决定将它加⼊  Scottish
Mortgage 的投资组合？
Lawrence Burns ：
我认为主要有三个原因。
第⼀个原因，是去年 12 ⽉我第⼀次⻅到  IO 时的感受。
我们对他介绍的情况，以及中国  AI ⽣态的发展都⾮常感兴趣。
按照  IO 的说法， MiniMax 已经能够以领先前沿模型⼗分之⼀的成本，实现约 95% 的性能。

其次，我们⾮常欣赏这种成本优势是如何融⼊公司基因的。
这种优势是在资源稀缺的环境中形成的，⽽这种起点对公司发展⽅式产⽣了深刻影响，也让它获
得了⼀种⽐我们最初认识到的更加结构性的优势。
正如你刚才提到的， MiniMax 并没有获得全球最先进  GPU 的同等资源。
因此， IO 从创⽴公司之初，就把重点放在如何从每⼀⼩时的计算资源中榨取更多有⽤的⼯作成
果上。
他们让芯⽚始终保持⾼利⽤率，并且从模型设计之初就把成本因素考虑进去。
这⾮常吸引我们。
因为这种低成本模式与我们在美国看到的模式有所不同，⽽且它在全球范围内可能具有⾮常⾼的
价值。
⾸先，并不是所有⼯作都需要使⽤最顶尖的模型。
其次，并不是所有⽤⼾和企业都像美国和欧洲的⽤⼾、企业那样富有，能够负担⾼昂的费⽤。
第⼆个原因，是  IO 本⼈。

### Page 24

他是⼀位备受尊敬的  AI 科学家，同时也被认为是⼀位⾮常优秀的资本配置者。
科学家和优秀资本配置者这两种能⼒集中在同⼀个⼈⾝上，是相当罕⻅的组合。
因此，我们认为他值得投资。
第三个原因是， AI 正在以极快的速度发展。
⽬前，⽆论是在美国加利福尼亚，还是在中国，都有⾮常有意思的技术进展。
我认为，如果你能够同时与这两个⽣态系统保持接触，就更有可能理解  AI 未来究竟会如何发
展。
如果你只观察其中⼀个⽣态系统，这项⼯作就会变得更加困难。
所以，我们认为，有必要在中国市场建⽴⼀定的投资敞⼝，以便学习和理解那⾥正在发⽣的事
情。
当然， MiniMax 给我们带来的股价表现也经历了⼀些波动。
正如你所说，我们在 1 ⽉份参与了公司的  IPO ，最初投资了⼤约 2000 万英镑。
公司上市后，股价出现了⾮常强劲的上涨。
这让我们有机会卖出价值 4000 万英镑的股票，相当于最初投资⾦额的两倍，同时继续持有公司
的股份。
以英镑计价，我们⽬前保留的持仓规模与最初投⼊的⾦额⼤致相同，仍然是 2000 万英镑左右。

所以，对我们⽽⾔，这是⼀笔不错的投资，也提供了⼀次⾮常难得的学习机会。
IO 的特殊之处：既是优秀科学家，也是资本配置者
Claire Shaw ：
这⾮常有帮助， Lawrence ，谢谢你提供这些背景信息。
我觉得⾮常值得注意的是， IO 在谈论⾃⼰的⽬标时，甚⾄将其与改变⼈类对宇宙的理解联系起
来。
在刚才的访谈中，你将  MiniMax 的企业⽂化概括为扁平、专注和节俭，并指出这种⽂化是他在
资源约束下追求宏⼤⽬标的产物。
我们不妨进⼀步深⼊讨论⼀下。
在你看来， IO 本⼈以及他所建⽴的企业⽂化，究竟通过哪些⽅式提⾼了  MiniMax 成功的概率？
Lawrence Burns ：
我还是想回到刚才提到的那个特点：
IO 既是⼀位优秀的  AI 科学家，也是⼀位⾮常出⾊的资本配置者。

### Page 25

观察整个  AI 实验室⾏业，尤其是美国的  AI 实验室，你会发现，它们在正式训练模型之前，往
往会投⼊⼤量资⾦开展各种实验，以判断哪些技术路线可⾏，哪些不可⾏。
它们拥有⾜够的资本和芯⽚资源，可以⽀持这样的做法。
但  IO 的重要性在于，他有能⼒成功缩⼩实验范围。
他会减少需要尝试的技术路线，作出更少但更重要的选择，从⽽提⾼资本使⽤效率，同时仍然取
得良好的结果。
因此，能够作出这些重⼤的技术决策就变得⾮常重要。
我们曾经与中国  AI ⽣态中⼀些⼯作多年的业内⼈⼠交流，他们认为，这正是  IO 的优势之⼀。
他能够⽐其他⼈更准确地判断技术将朝什么⽅向发展。
我认为，他作为科学家的声誉，加上他在资本配置⽅⾯的能⼒，也使他能够吸引中国顶尖的  AI
科学家加⼊团队。
正如我们从加利福尼亚以及美国市场了解到的那样， AI ⼈才⾮常稀缺，也极具价值。
因此，如果⼀家公司在吸引和留住⼈才⽅⾯拥有更强的能⼒，这最终就会成为⾮常重要的竞争优
势。
低成本推理的第⼆层价值：让模型拥有更多训练机会

Claire Shaw ：
接下来，我想进⼀步讨论  MiniMax 的商业模式。
我们前⾯谈到了公司如何将资源约束转化为竞争优势。
正如你所说，通过降低每⼀次模型回答问题的成本，公司就能够向客⼾提供更低的价格，从⽽赢
得更多业务。
但  IO 还提出了第⼆个不那么直观、却同样重要的观点：
通过保持低成本， MiniMax 可以承担更多模型初次训练完成之后的后续⼯作，⽽他认为，这个
阶段对  AI 技术进步正变得越来越重要。
在你看来，这⼀点对于  MiniMax 的投资逻辑有多重要？
Lawrence Burns ：
低成本战略是  MiniMax 所有⼯作的核⼼，⽽你刚才提到的第⼆层价值同样⾮常重要。
我们可以⽤⼀种⾮常简单的⽅式理解  AI 模型的训练过程。
⾸先，模型需要消化⼤量数据，从中获得知识，然后通过训练掌握这些知识。
接下来，还有⼀个叫作后训练（ Post-training ）的阶段。

### Page 26

在这个阶段，模型会反复尝试完成各种任务，不断练习，并从中学习什么⽅法有效、什么⽅法⽆
效。
过去⼏年， AI 领域很多重⼤的能⼒跃迁，都来⾃后训练阶段。
例如，模型现在能够编写代码、执⾏复杂任务，这些能⼒都与后训练密切相关。
所以，如果把这个阶段理解为模型的练习过程，那么它既昂贵，⼜⾮常重要。
因为模型每练习⼀次，就需要进⾏⼀次推理，也就会产⽣相应的成本。
我可以⽤⾼尔夫来打个⽐⽅。
假设有两名⾼尔夫球⼿，他们显然都会随着练习次数增加⽽不断进步。
但其中⼀名球⼿购买练习球的成本要⽐另⼀名低得多。
在其他条件相同的情况下，能够以更低成本获得更多练习球的⼈，通常会成为更优秀的球⼿。
MiniMax 就是这个例⼦中的那名球⼿。
MiniMax 拥有更便宜的练习球，因此同样的预算能够发挥更⼤的作⽤。
这就是低成本战略的核⼼价值。
因此，低成本回答不仅能够降低消费者和企业使⽤产品时的推理费⽤，还能够让产品本⾝得到更
多训练，以更快或者更具成本效益的⽅式持续改进。
Claire Shaw ：
很好，我很喜欢你这个⾼尔夫的⽐喻， Lawrence 。

盈利挑战：价格竞争与前沿研发成本不断上升
Claire Shaw ：
接下来，我们稍微转换⼀下话题，谈谈⻛险。
⽆论  MiniMax 的增⻓速度有多快，如果我们不认真考虑公司⾯临的⻛险，就没有尽到⾃⼰的职
责。
其中⼀个⻛险是，公司⽬前仍然处于亏损状态。
IO 认为， MiniMax 最终能够通过让收⼊增⻓速度超过成本增⻓速度来实现盈利。
但你认为，公司在实现盈利的过程中会⾯临哪些挑战？它⼜具备哪些条件来克服这些困难？
Lawrence Burns ：
我认为，这确实是⼀项挑战，我们不应该回避这⼀点。
今年上半年，公司的收⼊接近增⻓了四倍，但报告期内的亏损规模也达到了原来的⼤约三倍。

### Page 27

⻓期关注  AI 实验室公司的投资者会发现，这种投资和⽀出规模在⾏业内并不罕⻅。
但最终，公司仍然必须建⽴⼀个能够盈利的商业模式。
我认为，实现盈利有两个真正的挑战。
第⼀个挑战是中国市场极其激烈的价格竞争。
MiniMax 正在与  DeepSeek 等公司竞争，这并不容易。
第⼆个挑战是，想要始终保持在技术前沿，所需要的成本正在不断上升。
这也是  MiniMax 在 7 ⽉份融资 20 亿美元的原因。
那么，公司应该如何克服这些挑战？
我认为，答案的⼀部分就在于我们前⾯讨论过的⼏个因素：
保持精简的成本结构；
作出正确的技术选择；
在能够实现收⼊⼤幅增⻓的市场中扩⼤业务。
我认为，真正关键的是，随着模型能⼒不断提升，公司能够进⼊的市场也会越来越⼤。
⽬前，我们已经看到研发⽀出的增⻓速度开始低于销售收⼊的增⻓速度，这是⼀个令⼈⿎舞的信
号。
另外，我们也看到，公司的销售收⼊正越来越多地从消费者业务转向企业业务。
我认为，这将越来越有利于商业化变现。
因为随着模型能⼒提升，企业能够利⽤它完成更多⼯作，也就更容易愿意为这些能⼒⽀付更⾼的

费⽤。
MiniMax 在这⼀⽅⾯已经取得了⾮常强劲的进展。
我认为，企业业务收⼊增⻓了⼤约⼋倍，⽬前已经占到总收⼊的 63% 。
我想  IO 提到的数字是，按照年化运⾏率计算，企业业务占⽐已经超过 80% 。
因此，你可以看到，这种业务结构的变化正在实际发⽣。
但我认为，我们必须⾮常明确地认识到：
如果从整体上看， MiniMax 仍然是⼀项早期阶段的投资。
它具有⾮常明显的⾮对称性。
有很多事情可能朝着有利的⽅向发展，但最终结果的分布范围也⾮常宽。

### Page 28

这也是我们在投资组合中控制其持仓规模的部分原因。
MiniMax 的市场空间究竟有多⼤？
Claire Shaw ：
最后，我们来讨论⼀个问题。
IO 提到，提供更强⼤的  AI Agent ，并将  MiniMax 最好的语⾔模型和视频模型整合到⼀个系统
中，将打开更⼤的市场机会。
但我们都知道，这是⼀个变化极快的⾏业，因此很难准确判断未来会发⽣什么，以及这些变化将
在什么时候出现。
作为⼀名⻓期投资者，你认为  MiniMax 所提供的服务，未来能够形成多⼤的市场？
Lawrence Burns ：
对于这项投资，我有⼀个问题并不担⼼，那就是：
市场规模是否⾜够⼤？
我认为，只要公司能够成功实现⾃⼰的⽬标，市场显然⾜够⼤。
如果你能够以低成本提供越来越强⼤的智能，就⼀定能够找到市场。
⽽且，随着产品能⼒不断提升，市场规模也会持续扩⼤。
你刚才提到了语⾔模型与视频模型的整合，这就是⼀个很好的例⼦。
因为⼤多数⼯作实际上都需要多种不同技能的结合，⽽不是只需要⼀种技能。
今天的视频模型擅⻓⽣成视频⽚段，但它们并不擅⻓接受复杂指令。

它们也⽆法像专业视频剪辑师那样，根据要求对已经⽣成的视频进⾏修改。
这就需要语⾔模型所具备的理解能⼒。
⼀旦⼀个系统能够同时完成这两件事， AI 的应⽤就会从简单地⽣成视频⽚段，升级为能够承担
⼴告、电影和设计领域的完整项⽬。
⽽⼈们愿意为这样的智能和能⼒⽀付⾼得多的费⽤。
因此，我们预计，这⼀领域的需求将随着新模型的推出⽽逐步释放。
例如， IO 刚才介绍的实时视频⽣成能⼒，就是⼀个可能推动市场扩⼤的新应⽤⽅向。
所以，我认为，这项投资真正需要关注的重⼤问题，是竞争、盈利能⼒和利润率，⽽不是市场规
模。
我们对这个市场最终能够变得⾮常庞⼤，还是相当有信⼼的。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

提醒⼀下，我们还将像之前的节⽬⼀样，推出这次访谈的 10 分钟精华剪辑，⽅便⼤家在时间有
限时快速了解核⼼内容。
另外，我们也已经在节⽬说明中解释了所有专业术语。
本季节⽬到这⾥就结束了，但我们将在 2027 年继续推出更多独家访谈，邀请我们投资组合企业
的领导者参与。
如果你希望进⼀步了解  Scottish Mortgage ，或者收听之前错过的节⽬，可以访问我们的⽹站。

## Document 411: Swing State_ Navigating Midterm Volatility (2026-10-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Swing State_ Navigating Midterm Volatility (2026-10-23).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Swing State: Navigating 
Midterm Volatility

1 
Authors 
Laura Daniels 
Deputy Associate Director of Equity  
Research

Josh Ray 
Associate Director of Equity 
Research

Matthew Barnard 
Head of US Company Research

Parag Thatte 
Strategist

Brett Ryan 
Senior US Economist

October 23, 2026 
A client-facing version of this report is available at the Deutsche Bank Research 
Portal. The following Deutsche Bank Research analysts contributed to this report: 
Brett Ryan (econ), Binky Chadha and Parag Thatte (equity strategy), Benjamin 
Soff (data centers), Daniel Knauff (software), George Hill (healthcare), Omotayo 
Okusanya (REITs), Peter Abramowitz (office REITs), Mark DeVries (consumer and 
specialty finance), Nicole DeBlase (MI/EE), Corinne Blanchard (clean tech), 
Richa Harnain (transports), and Scott Deuschle (aerospace and defense).  
With the 2026 US midterm elections approaching, we look at key investor 
debates surrounding the event and legislative priorities of the next Congress. 
Despite the uncertainty that midterms can bring, the market appears fairly 
confident on expectations for the overall results. While a surprise Republican-
majority victory in both chambers is still possible, consensus is converging 
around current polling indicating Democrats are likely to flip the seats needed for 
a House majority and may win a Senate majority. Historically, an incumbent 
president has lost seats in 20 of the last 22 midterms. Investor expectations are 
in line with this. Polymarket, for example, is currently above 90% for Democrats 
gaining control of the House and above 60% for Democrats gaining control of the 
Senate.  
With this set of expectations, key investor debates instead focus on the 
legislative agenda for hot-ticket policy issues. Namely, these hinge on AI and 
data centers, affordability (affecting healthcare, housing, and education loans), 
and economic and fiscal policy. Debates also surround possible influence of the 
new Congress on the war in Iran and, by extension, energy prices. In this report, 
we cut through the noise on what the next Congress could reasonably advance 
from these agendas, what the impact would be on affected sectors, and how to 
address market uncertainty – and perceived certainty – into the elections. 
Historically, midterm elections have proven to be a check on administration 
policies. The number of seats lost in the midterms by the incumbent party is tied 
to the president’s approval rating going into midterms, which in turn tends to 
align with consumer confidence. President Trump’s ~38% approval rating, at a 
record low for his tenure, would suggest a little over a 45-seat loss for the 
Republican Party (in the House and Senate combined). That said, polling in the 
Senate races that will determine control of the chamber largely remains within 
the margin of error, and in 2024, Trump won in at least five of the seven core toss-
up states. Thus, the Senate remains a much closer call than the House race.

### Page 2

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: Presidential Approval vs. House Seats

Source: Real Clear Politics

Figure 2: Presidential Approval vs. Consumer Confidence

Source: The Conference Board

Whatever the outcome of the  midterms, the legislative agenda will 
still face constraints from the White House. It is all but impossible for either party 
to gain a veto -proof majority  in either chamber, minimizing the likelihood of a 
large shift in fiscal or regulatory policy. The most  immediately obvious impact of 
Democrats taking the Senate would be the ability to slow or block executive 
branch appointees, including at the Federal Reserve Board of Governors or the 
Supreme Court, should any members step down before their terms end.

### Page 3

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Without garnering a two-thirds majority in both chambers, Democrats lack a clear 
path to unwind the 2025 tax cuts or enact AI-related regulatory legislation – at 
least, not without President Trump’s explicit approval. With respect to the near -
term fiscal policy outlook, the most important period for investors may be the 
“lame duck” session, the period after the November 3 election and before the new 
Congress convenes on January 3. Recall that current federal government funding 
expires on December 11, and the Trump administration is asking for a nearly 60% 
increase in defense spending, the largest since the Korean War.  In addition, the 
debt limit will become binding again around the middle of next year. In short, the 
actions during the lame duck session could determine whether the new Congress 
is seated amid a government shutdown, a contentious battle over the federal 
budget, and the debt limit looming in the background.  
In terms of the market reaction to midterm  elections, the risk-reward profile has 
typically been favorable , with 21 of the last 23 midterm years  seeing positive 
returns in the fourth quarter, with an average gain of 7% in the S&P. The gains are 
notable even after adjusting for typical positive year-end seasonality.

Figure 3: S&P Returns and Midterms

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP

It w ould not be unusual for the market to go sideways as investors hedge 
potential volatility and uncertainty, as we have seen previously ahead of other 
well-telegraphed pivotal events. This has historically been followed by a rally as 
the results became clear and  uncertainty dissipated, regardless of the result 
itself. In the current context, if election polls and prediction market probabilities 
continue being lopsided, the market may not treat the election as an uncertain 
risk event going in. Uncertainty could still ramp up after, and the typical midterm 
election rally may be pushed out. It is notable that the one prominent exception 
to the midterm rallies was in 2018 , when the market sold off sharply after the 
elections as uncertainty spiked around trade war 1.0 as well as Fed policy.

### Page 4

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

We note that the midterm elections also coincide with the third quarter earnings 
season, during which we expect companies to deliver growth of 34%. For next 
year, we expect growth to be a still -robust 17% with risks to the upside. 
Eventually, earnings tend to be the anchor for equities and have been for several 
years despite numerous domestic political as well as geopolitical shocks. 
Across sectors, most have rallied around past midterms in keeping with the 
market, but there isn’t a consistent pattern of out - or underperformance . This 
suggests that for the initial conditions for each sector, specific policies and 
positioning played a bigger role.  For that reason, we turn to a closer analysis of 
key legislative and policy debates impacting exposed sectors.

### Page 5

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

AI and Data Centers  
AI and data centers have become a campaign talking point for candidates from 
both the Democratic and Republican parties. This has spurred investor questions 
on whether to expect a national regulatory framework constraining data center 
buildouts. Some of th e headlines surrounding this debate overestimate what 
Congress can  accomplish through national legislation compared with what is 
possible at a state level, particularly during a presidential administration 
supportive of AI and data center acceleration. Still, the industry is responding to 
a political backdrop that is increasingly scrutinizing AI and data centers.  
The current administration has prioritized advancing AI and digital infrastructure 
development. The White House ’s AI Action Plan , released July 2025, included a 
set of executive orders supporting the rapid buildout of AI data centers by 
reducing certain federal regulatory and permitting burdens. The administration 
did not preempt state laws on data center infrastructure development and 
federal permitting orders generally do not override state zoning and utility rules. 
In March 2026 , the White House announced the Ratepayer Protection Pledge, 
under which large data center operators would voluntarily commit to covering 
the cost to upgrade generation, transmission, substations, or other necessary 
infrastructure to service these developments.  The industry’s response to these 
proposals has been largely constructive. The Data Center Coalition  (DCC), an 
industry group representing major  hyperscalers and data center  owners and 
operators came out in support of the initial version of the Pledge, while arguing 
that large -load customers across all industries also should cover the costs to 
upgrade energy infrastructure. According to the DCC , “the data center 
industry remains committed to being responsible partners where we  operate, 
which includes  paying our full energy costs  and investing in local jobs and 
workforce development.” 
Congress voted in September 2026 to codify the principles present in the Pledge 
via the Ratepayer Protection Act. The bill, which only required states to “consider” 
these standards, passed overwhelmingly in the House (417-3), but failed to reach 
60 votes in the Senate (57 -43). Critics noted the bill stopped short of imposing 
mandatory nationwide rules. 
Looking forward, we expect a Congress that is more focused on consumer 
protection might look to codify a more stringent version of these rules, requiring 
large-load customers to bear the full incremental costs associated with 
generation, transmission, and distribution upgrades.  However, even if such a 
framework were passed into law, we believe the overall economic impact to large 
data center operators would be relatively modest, for two reasons. 
For one, the White House would maintain a veto over  bills that include language 
it views as overly burdensome towards AI infrastructure development . Two, data 
center operators  generally underwrite projects only when they have a high 
degree of confidence in securing power and, where necessary, may already be 
embedding the cost of upgrading energy infrastructure into their investment 
plans. We expect developers to be capable of passing on sustained increases in 
power infrastructure costs to their customers , which, for many 100  MW+ 
developments, are the major investment-grade hyperscalers.

### Page 6

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 6

Though data center development is receiving increasing national attention, it is 
still largely a local issue, and the most consequential elections will be at the state 
and local levels. We have seen a number of state -driven actions on data center s 
in recent months, including: 
• In July, New York State’s governor issued an executive order to establish 
a temporary moratorium on data center development  while the state 
develops a standardized framework of rules for key issues (including 
responsibilities for power and energy infrastructure upgrades; additional 
power generation; clean energy; water use, emission, and waste 
management; and state/local financ ial incentives). The New York 
legislature also passed a one -year moratorium on Department of 
Environmental Conservation permits and approvals for 20  MW+ data 
center developments. 
• In August, Texas’ governor directed the state’s public utility commission 
to conduct a comprehensive audit of every data center advancing 
through the ERCOT (Texas’s grid operator) interconnection process. The 
audit will cover several notable areas including public financial support, 
electricity demand and supply, water use, community impacts, and 
ownership and control. 
• In September, California’s governor passed a comprehensive regulatory 
package, covering electricity, water, reporting, and environmental 
review. Most of these provisions take effect January 1, 2027, and phase 
in through 2027 -2028, but the package does not ban data centers or 
impose a statewide moratorium on development activity. 
• Oregon, Pennsylvania, New Jersey, and other states have also 
established orders to create standards and frameworks governing data 
center development (but without imposing broad moratoriums). 
At the more local level, a large number of moratoriums have been passed on new 
data center development. Data collected by SemiAnalysis, an independent 
research firm, shows that, as of September, more than 300 local data  center 
moratoriums have been adopted nationwide across counties, municipalities, 
townships, and other local governments.  
However, these state and local moratoriums appear  thus far to be less impactful 
to near- and medium-term development activity than headline coverage might 
otherwise suggest. According to SemiAnalysis, only about 2.3 GW of the 38 GW 
of data center capacity planned or under construction for 2027 has faced delays 
as a result of  the New York state executive order (0.8 GW) and/or local 
moratoriums (1.5 GW). 
We believe the primary reasons that moratoriums are, in reality, less likely to 
impact near -term development activity are (1) that these moratoriums largely 
impact new projects and applications (rather than existing developments in  
progress), (2) there may be flexibility to relocate or rezone early-stage projects in 
response to moratoriums, and (3) data center developers are increasingly 
partnering with local communities to gain their support via investments and other 
programs (as we wrote about recently).  Potential regulatory risk has not yet 
slowed capex spending patterns, with capex spend still expected to climb from

### Page 7

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 7

$0.5 trillion in 2025 to $1.3 trillion by 2027; data centers typically represent ~20-
30% of the total bill of materials for new AI capacity.  
Although the practical impact on near- and medium-term development pipelines 
appears less pronounced than headlines might suggest, all this is not to say that 
data centers will not face increasing local opposition following the midterms. 
Indeed, i n our conversations with industry participants, there is an 
acknowledgment that greater public scrutiny of data centers is not going away.  
Additional moratoriums, zoning restrictions, permitting requirements, or utility -
related conditions could affect the pace, cost , and overall feasibility of future 
projects.

### Page 8

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 8

Affordability Agenda: Healthcare, Housing, and 
Education Loans  
The affordability agenda has become a centerpiece of many Democratic as well 
as Republican campaign platforms.  This platform is impacting investor 
debates across healthcare, housing, and student loans. In healthcare,  the push 
among some Democrats for expanded health coverage under the Medicare for All 
banner is placing pressure on managed care stocks, but meanwhile, companies 
with exposure to Medicaid and  the Affordable Care Act (ACA) could see a 
supportive political backdrop. On housing, investor debates on the impacts of the 
affordability agenda have continued since passage of legislation in July 2026 that 
caps the number of single -family homes for -profit institutional investors can 
purchase. Affordability of higher education is likewise part of the conversatio n, 
leading investors to debate which levers Congress could pull that would affect 
student loan companies.  
Healthcare 
In healthcare, w e see the upcoming midterm elections as a sentiment event for 
managed care stocks , not a policy event.  With the presidential veto in place 
through 2028, we do not expect the government to enact direct healthcare 
legislation during the next Congress. Forecasts place the probability of a change 
in House control at roughly 79%, with both chambers changing in about 44% of 
simulations. Although investor skittishness around Medicare for All (M4A) rhetoric 
is high, we see the likelihood of M4A replacing private coverage as a near -zero 
probability in this timeframe.  
M4A has no realistic legislative path before 2029, but the rhetoric weighs on 
managed care organization (MCO) stock multiples by raising perceived long-term 
risk rather than affecting near -term earnings. Polling from  Kaiser Family 
Foundation, a health policy research organization, shows support for M4A at a 
decade-plus high, and the 2018 –2019 cycle suggests that increased campaign 
attention can pressure MCO multiples even without a direct policy impact. We 
expect similar sentiment -driven pressure through year -end and potentially into 
the 2028 primary cycle but would not view it as a change i n near -term 
fundamentals.  
For Medicare Advantage, we see a h eadline risk over earnings reality. The near-
term earnings impact for affected companies should be limited, because the US 
Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) has finalized a 2.48% Medicare 
Advantage (MA) rate increase for 2027, versus 0.09% in the advance notice. A 
change in House control would instead increase focus on 2028 payment policy, 
risk adj ustment, and prior authorization. Coding reform already has bipartisan 
support: sponsors estimate that the Cassidy -Merkley No UPCODE Act could 
generate up to $275  billion of savings over ten years, while MedPAC estimates 
coding intensity will add roughly $40  billion to 2025 Medicare Advantage  costs 
relative to traditional Medicare. In our view, the more immediate consideration is 
potential multiple pressure rather than a measurable change to company 
earnings.  
However, the next Congress could b enefit Medicaid funding and ACA policy. 
Medicaid and the ACA appear relatively better positioned, although any benefit

### Page 9

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 9

would likely be reflected first in sentiment and lower perceived policy risk rather 
than near -term estimate revisions. Medicaid community engagement 
requirements take effect in most states by January 1, 2027. Although repeal 
remains unlikely given the presidential veto, a change in House control could shift 
the year -end funding debate toward  restoring or  preserving federal Medicaid 
support, which could ease state budget pressure and support rate -setting. The 
ACA policy backdrop could also become more supportive, although we see 
limited scope for enhanced premium subsidies to return before 2029 after the 
three-year extension passed by the House stalled.  
Housing 
Turning to the housing sector, the likely midterm outcome of a split government 
would result in more gridlock. Historically, political gridlock in terms of passing 
new regulation has been a tailwind for the real estate sector, reducing regulatory 
risk at the federal level. In the scenario of a split Congress, in which the Democrats 
have a majority in the House but not the Senate, bills with bipartisan support have 
a greater likelihood to become law. Two key areas in which this bipartisan support 
exists are housing affordability and consumer protection. 
Among real estate investment trusts ( REITs), we see Residential as among the 
most impacted subsectors, particularly as it relates to the affordability debate. 
Regardless of the election outcome, housing affordability has become front and 
center on the political agenda  across both parties. F ederal legislation  is more 
likely to target supply -side solutions, as legislation restricting rent increases is 
typically handled at the local and state level. We think Multifamily would be the 
most likely subsector impacted, as a push to expand rental housing supply could 
lead to eased zoning restrictions, particularly in supply -constrained coastal 
markets that have historically benefited from higher barriers to entry. That said, 
this would likely take years to materialize. With a Democratic majority , Congress 
could voice opposition to the influence of large institutional landlords, but we see 
this strictly as a sentiment risk to stocks.  
Single Family For Rent (SFR) is less exposed to near -term legislative shocks, as 
the sector was a focus of the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act passed in July 
2026 over the summer. The  measure impacts institutional owners’ ability to buy 
homes off the MLS but contains specific carve-out provisions that will allow them 
to keep developing build -to-rent (BTR) product and buy excess inventory from 
homebuilders, the sector's preferred acquis ition channel in recent years. The 
ROAD to Housing Act also contained provisions that could theoretically ease the 
production of manufactured homes, the main business segment for 
Manufactured Housing (MH) REITs. The first-order impact is likely more flexibility 
in home designs that could lead to further densification of MH communities, 
although we note emerging investor debate about the long -term impacts of 
increased MH production on competitive supply for a property type t hat has 
historically been supply constrained, mainly due to NIMBY-ism.  
Parallel to the conversation on affordability, it is clear that, regardless of which 
political party may be in power following the midterms, a major focus remains 
addressing the US fiscal deficit and its continued growth. We continue to pay 
attention to potential budget cuts or sequestration to slow government spending 
and reduce the deficit, which could have an impact on real estate.  Key areas to 
watch include cuts to healthcare spending (which would be a negative for skilled

### Page 10

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 10

nursing focused REITs, given Medicare is a major revenue source for their tenant 
base) and cuts that would impact Office REITs with large government exposure.  
We also worry that an elevated federal deficit and the elevated Treasury 
borrowing needs today create a risk that interest rates will remain  higher for 
longer. This puts pressure on real estate valuations and cost of financing for REITs 
to drive external growth via acquisitions and development. A split Congress is 
likely to result in more difficult budget negotiations and increased oversight, but 
it remains unclear w hat the ultimate outcome would be to address the budget 
deficit issue. 
With real estate being more dependent on local market dynamics, we believe the 
more likely regulatory impact would come from local regulation. This could show 
up on the ballots during the midterms , with increased possibility of rent control 
legislation targeted at residential real estate as well as self -storage, as cities and 
municipalities focus on affordability issues. There is also growing legislative 
pushback at the local level against development of real assets such as data 
centers given the large power requirements, as discussed in detail elsewhere in 
this report.  
Education Lending 
Midterm campaign platforms on education affordability  have a bearing on the 
consumer and specialty finance sector. Much of the policy exposure in this sector 
is through the executive branch bodies, not Congress, however. This includes, for 
example, regulatory authorities  such as the FDIC (Federal Deposit Insurance 
Corporation), the CFPB (Consumer Financial Protection Bureau ), or the FHFA 
(Federal Housing Finance Agency, which regulates Fannie Mae and Freddie 
Mac). That said, historical data shows that student loan company stocks can still 
directionally align with midterm party outcome and expectations  in Congress, 
although the sample size is small.

### Page 11

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 11

Mixed Impacts of Economic and Fiscal Policy  
Democratic and Republican candidates have aimed to distinguish themselves 
from their opponents along a range of economic and fiscal policy matters, 
including tariffs, tax, labor, and the budget. In reality, the outcome of the 
midterms, regardless of which party prevails, is likely to present a mixture of relief 
and restrictions affecting businesses. In some cases, investor fears over sweeping 
economic policy changes are overblown for topics over which Congress 
realistically has limited influence, presenting more headline  risk than a risk to 
fundamentals. In considering these, we look to industrials that are particularly 
sensitive to key investor debates , spanning multi-industry/electrical equipment, 
clean tech and alternative energy, transport, and defense.  
For the multi-industry and electrical equipment (MI/EE) sector, historical midterm 
election cycles suggest a generally constructive backdrop heading into election 
day, with positive returns in six of the past seven elections. On average, the MI/EE 
group has gained ~5% during the three months preceding historical midterm 
elections and ~8% during the final month prior. Relative outperformance v ersus 
the S&P 500 has been less consistent; we observed outperformance in only four 
of seven cycles, though the group still generated average excess returns of 
~250bps over the preceding three months and ~270bps over the preceding 
month.  
Overall, the data supports a favorable absolute return backdrop ahead of 
midterms, but significant stock -level dispersion suggests company -specific 
fundamentals and factor leadership have historically mattered more than the 
election cycle alone. We mapped out election results scenarios across key policy 
pillars for our coverage (tax policy and capital investment; trade, tariffs, and 
supply chains; energy, grid, and power infrastructure; AI and data center 
regulation; and the regulatory environment). We focus on two key scenarios: one 
in which Republicans maintain control of both chambers, and a divided 
government that sees the Democrats controlling one or both chambers of the 
legislative branch.  
Under a divided government, we expect stock -level dispersion would likely 
narrow, with globally exposed companies receiving some trade policy relief and 
HVAC/automation companies benefiting from a potentially more supportive 
regulatory backdrop. However, should Republicans maintain control of Congress, 
our scenario analysis suggests this would be favorable for electrical equipment 
and power infrastructure manufacturers, due to a combination of pro-investment 
tax policy, faster infrastructure deployment, and a lighter AI regulatory 
framework. That said, globally exposed companies could face incremental tariff 
and retaliation risks.  
Looking to the clean tech and alternative energy sector, the midterm elections 
could represent a positive tailwind . T he next Congress might be positioned to 
pursue targeted extensions or modifications to certain clean -energy tax credits. 
The introduction of H.R. 8477, which proposes to restore several clean energy tax 
credits rolled back in late 2025, seems to signal tha t some, albeit a limited 
number, of Republicans could be supportive of this initiative. If the Democrats 
gain a majority in the House but not the Senate in the midterms, it is plausible that 
Democrats could find sufficient  Republican support to advance

### Page 12

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 12

legislation extending clean -energy credits, though they would still face 
significant legislative hurdles. A midterm outcome in which Republicans retain 
control of both chambers is likely to maintain the status quo  for the remaining 
existing tax credits, especially for those tied to domestic manufacturing. While 
those could potentially see further extension, we think this is a limited outcome.    
While we see a low probability of a major change in, or support for, the residential 
tax credits that expired at the end of 2025 (25D), we envision a scenario in which 
indirect residential tax credits (48E) could be extended, which would help boost 
part of the demand. Softness  in demand is expected to remain going into 2027, 
still impacted by the phaseout of the 25D tax credits, the eventual roll-off of safe-
harbor benefits, and a more constrained financing environment.  
From a manufacturing tax credit standpoint,  there could be additional tailwinds  
if we were to see any manufacturing tax credit extension or positive modification 
(although lower probability, in our view) . This would also likely improve utility -
scale project economics if broader project -related credits are preserved, though 
upcoming elections could cause a slowdown in new booking activity until 
legislative uncertainty clears.  
For Transports,  the midterms would be most relevant for the trucking supply 
thesis. Truckload stocks could have a modest negative reaction to a Democratic 
majority if investors expect some rollback of the Trump administration’s 
constraints on drivers , which has tightened supply . We would separate 
perception from fundamentals, however. The election of a Democratic Congress 
would not automatically by itself change leadership at the Department of 
Transportation (DOT) and the Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration 
(FMCSA), the executive branch entities driving much of the current push  for 
restrictions on drivers.  
One possible impact of  the midterms for  Transports is if  a change in 
Congressional control  contributed to a faster resolution of the war in Iran . Any 
resulting lower energy prices would be a positive for Transports . We view this as 
a lower -probability outcome,  however, given the constraints on a divided 
government.  
With clashes over the budget likely looming, aerospace and def ense could get 
caught in the crosshairs. W hile defense spending has historically enjoyed 
bipartisan support, it has grown politicized following the war in Iran, making it 
more difficult for a split Congress to pass such a budget. The defense budget has 
experienced significant growth over the last few years, rising from $900 billion in 
FY2024 to $1 trillion in FY2026. The Trump administration would like to see this 
trend accelerate: in April 2026, the president issued a budget request that called 
for a $1.5  trillion budget for the Department of Defense in FY 2027. With a 
continuing resolution likely into year-end, the outcome of the midterm elections 
will determine whether the next Congress can pass such a budget. If Republicans 
hold the House and Senate, we think there  is a reasonable prospect of a budget 
of $1.2 trillion or more  passing into law . Defense investors would see that as a 
substantial tailwind for beaten-up defense stocks. On the other hand, this would 
be unlikely in a split Congress, in our view.  Given all this, we see the outcome of 
the upcoming midterm elections as key to determining how US defense stocks 
will perform into year end and beyond.

### Page 13

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 13

Taking Stock of the Known Unknowns 
Although Republicans could maintain control of both chambers,  consensus is 
converging around polls pointing to a Democratic majority in the House  and 
possibly the Senate. The perceived certainty has allowed the investor debate 
related to midterms to focus not only on who will win but how they will govern. In 
some cases, headlines are misrepresenting what a Congress constrained by the 
executive will be able to accomplish . In other areas, legislation that is expected 
to be passed will need to be taken together with offsetting factors. Whatever the 
outcome, the political backdrop will continue to influence the market and 
investor debates in the year ahead.

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Swing State: Navigating Midterm Volatility – October 23, 2026
